for
j=1,\dots,q
.

We can now define our map
\Phi:\Omega\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{R}^{m}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}\to\mathfrak{M}
for a smaller
\Omega
, as

\Phi(t,x,s):=\exp_{T}(t,\exp_{X^{\prime\prime}}(x,\Gamma(s)))=e^{tT}e^{xX^{\prime\prime}}(\Gamma(s))=e^{t^{1}T_{1}}\dots e^{t^{r}T_{r}}e^{x^{1}X_{r+1}}\dots e^{x^{m}X_{r+m}}(0^{r+m},s^{1},\dots,s^{q}).

(3.15)

The notation
\exp_{T},\exp_{X^{\prime\prime}}
are given in Corollary
3.23
. By taking
\varepsilon_{0}
in Lemma
3.22
small enough we see that
\Phi
is defined is when
\Omega
is small enough.

We are going to see that
\Phi
and
F:=\Phi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
are the desired maps.

By shrinking
\Omega
if necessary, we can define an auxiliary map
\Psi:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}
as the following

\displaystyle\Psi(t,x,s):=\exp_{\tilde{X}}((t,x),(0,0,s))=e^{tX^{\prime}+xX^{\prime\prime}}(0,0,s)=e^{t^{1}X_{1}}\dots e^{t^{r}X_{r}}e^{x^{1}X_{r+1}}\dots e^{x^{m}X_{r+m}}(0^{r+m},s^{1},\dots,s^{q}).

(3.16)

(1)
and
(4)
are immediately since
\Phi(0)=\Psi(0)=(0,0,0)=p
and
F(p)=F(0)=(0,0,0)
.

By construction (
3.12
) (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), we know

\textstyle\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t}(0)=T(0)=\frac{\partial\Psi}{\partial t}(0)=X^{\prime}(0)=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}\big|_{p},\quad\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial x}(0)=\frac{\partial\Psi}{\partial x}(0)=X^{\prime\prime}(0)=\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\big|_{p},\quad\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial s}(0)=\frac{\partial\Psi}{\partial s}(0)=\frac{\partial\Gamma}{\partial s}(0)=\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\big|_{p}.

So
\nabla\Phi(0)
and
\nabla\Psi(0)
both have full rank
r+m+q
. By the Inverse Function Theorem and shrinking
\Omega
if necessary, we see that
\Phi:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}
and
\Psi:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}
are both
C^{1}
-regular parameterizations.

Therefore,
F=\Phi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
is well-defined coordinate chart on
U:=\Phi(\Omega)\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
. We write
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime})
as the components to
\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}
,
\mathbb{R}^{m}_{x}
and
\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
respectively.

We define
\tilde{U}:=\Psi(\Omega)
and
G:=\Psi^{\mathrm{Inv}}:\tilde{U}\to\mathbb{R}^{r+m}_{(t,x)}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
. Similarly, we write
G=(G^{\prime},G^{\prime\prime})
where
G^{\prime}:\tilde{U}\to\mathbb{R}^{r+m}_{(t,x)}
and
G^{\prime\prime}:\tilde{U}\to\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
.

Proof of
(2)
: By Corollary
3.23
we know
\exp_{T}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+1,\gamma}_{t,(u,v,w)}
and
\exp_{X^{\prime\prime}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1,\beta}_{x,(u,v,w)}
. By Lemma
2.32

(i)
, since
\Gamma\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
, we know
\exp_{X^{\prime\prime}}(x,\Gamma(s))\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1,\beta}_{x,s}
and thus
\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+1,\min(\gamma,\beta+1),\beta}_{t,x,s}
, in a neighborhood of
(0,0,0)
.

Similarly, using
\exp_{\tilde{X}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1,\beta}_{(t,x),(u,v,w)}
, we get
\Psi\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1,\beta}_{(t,x),s}
.

We have
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}=T_{j}\circ\Phi
,
j=1,\dots,r
. By Lemma
2.32

(i)
with
T\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
and
\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+1,\min(\gamma,\beta+1),\min(\gamma,\beta)}_{t,x,s}
we get
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t}=T\circ\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma,\min(\gamma,\beta+1),\min(\gamma,\beta)}_{t,x,s}
.

We have
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial x^{j}}(t,x,s)=((e^{tT})_{*}X_{j})\circ\Phi(t,x,s)
,
j=1,\dots,m
. For the pushforward formula we have

((e^{tT})_{*}X_{j})(u,v,w)=(X_{j}\cdot\nabla_{u,v,w}(e^{tT}))(e^{-tT}(u,v,w)).

(3.17)

We see that
(t,u,v,w)\mapsto(e^{tT}_{*}X_{j})(u,v,w)
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma-1,\beta)}
-map. By Lemma
2.32

(i)
we get
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial x}\in\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma-1,\beta)}
. Now the proof of
(2)
is complete.

Proof of
(6)
: Since
\Phi=F^{\mathrm{Inv}}
, we have
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{j}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}\circ F
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial x^{j}}\circ F
. By (
3.15
) we have
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}=T_{j}\circ\Phi
, so
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{j}}=T_{j}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(U;T\mathfrak{M})
for
j=1,\dots,r
. By
(2)
we know
\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma,\beta)}
and
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial x^{j}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma-1,\beta)}
, so
F\in\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma,\beta)}
and by composition we get
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma-1,\beta)}
for
j=1,\dots,m
.

Proof of
(3)
and
(7)
: Again by
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{i}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{i}}\circ\Phi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial x^{j}}\circ\Phi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
for
i=1,\dots,r
,
j=1,\dots,m
, we know
(3)
and
(7)
are equivalent. It suffices to prove
(7)
.

By (
3.15
) we have
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{i}}=T_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,r
. Since by (
3.13
)
T_{1},\dots,T_{r}
form a local basis of
\mathcal{V}
on
U\subseteq U_{1}
, we know
\mathcal{V}|_{U}
is spanned by
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}}
.

To prove
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{m}}
span
\mathcal{E}
in a neighborhood of
p=0\in\mathfrak{M}
, we need to show that

F^{\prime\prime\prime}=G^{\prime\prime},\quad\text{ in a neighborhood of }p=(0,0,0)\in\mathfrak{M}.

(3.18)

Once (
3.18
) is done, since
F
and
G
are both coordinate chart,
(dF^{\prime\prime\prime 1},\dots,dF^{\prime\prime\prime q})
and
(dG^{\prime\prime 1},\dots,dG^{\prime\prime q})
are both collection of
q
-differentials that are linearly independent at every point in the domain, we have that, in a neighborhood of
p\in\mathfrak{M}
,

\textstyle\operatorname{Span}(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x})=(\operatorname{Span}dF^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\bot}=(\operatorname{Span}dG^{\prime\prime})^{\bot}=\operatorname{Span}(G^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},G^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x})=\operatorname{Span}(X_{1},\dots,X_{r+m})=\mathcal{E}.

This would prove
(3)
by shrinking
\Omega
(and thus
U=\Phi(\Omega)
).

To prove (
3.18
), let
(t_{0},x_{0},s_{0})\in\Omega
be any point such that
\Phi(t_{0},x_{0},s_{0})
lays in the domain of
G
. It suffices to show
G^{\prime\prime}(t_{0},x_{0},s_{0})=s_{0}
. To see this, by (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) we have
\Phi(0,x_{0},s_{0})=\Psi(0,x_{0},s_{0})
, so
G^{\prime\prime}(\Phi(0,x_{0},s_{0}))=s_{0}
since
G=\Psi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
.

Now for
1\leq i\leq r
and for
t\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
small,
\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{i}}\Phi(t,x_{0},s_{0})=T_{i}\circ\Phi(t,x_{0},s_{0})
is the linear combination of
X_{1}|_{\Phi(t,x_{0},s_{0})},\dots,X_{r+m}|_{\Phi(t,x_{0},s_{0})}\in\mathcal{E}_{\Phi(t,x_{0},s_{0})}=(\operatorname{Span}dG^{\prime\prime})^{\bot}|_{\Phi(t,x_{0},s_{0})}
. Therefore
(\Phi_{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{i}})G^{\prime\prime j}\equiv 0
for
1\leq i\leq r
,
1\leq j\leq q
, in a neighborhood of
0\in\mathfrak{M}
, which means
t\mapsto G^{\prime\prime}(\Phi(t,x_{0},s_{0}))
is constant.

Since
G^{\prime\prime}(\Phi(0,x_{0},s_{0}))=s_{0}
and
t_{0}
is also small, we conclude that
G^{\prime\prime}(\Phi(t_{0},x_{0},s_{0}))=s_{0}
, completing the proof of (
3.18
) and hence of
(3)
.

Proof of
(5)
:
Clearly
F^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}(U;\mathbb{R}^{r})
, because by (
3.13
) and (
3.15
) we have

F^{\prime}(u,v,w)=u,\quad\text{ on }U\subseteq\mathfrak{M}.

(3.19)

Similarly,
G^{\prime}(u,v,w)=(u,v)
as well.

To see
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}(U;\mathbb{R}^{m})
, we write
F^{\prime\prime}
in terms of
\exp_{T}
. Here we denote
\tilde{v}:\mathfrak{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}_{v}
,
\tilde{v}(u,v,w):=v
as the natural projection
5
5

5

We abuse of notation here. It is more convenient to use
u,v,w
as variables in the proof. Thus we use
\tilde{v}
for
v
in (
3.12
).
. Using the proven fact
G^{\prime}(u,v,w)=(u,v)
we have
\tilde{v}(\Psi(0,x,s))=\tilde{v}(e^{xX^{\prime\prime}}(\Gamma(s)))=x
.

Note that by (
3.15
) and (
3.16
) we have
\Phi(0,x,s)=\Psi(0,x,s)
and
\Phi(0,x,s)=e^{-t\cdot T}(\Phi(t,x,s))
. Therefore

\displaystyle F^{\prime\prime}(\Phi(t,x,s))=x=\tilde{v}(\Phi(0,x,s))=\tilde{v}(e^{-t\cdot T}(\Phi(t,x,s))=\tilde{v}(e^{-F^{\prime}(\Phi(t,x,s))\cdot T}(\Phi(t,x,s)).

Using
(u,v,w)=\Phi(t,x,s)
and that
F^{\prime}(u,v,w)=u
from (
3.19
), we conclude that in the domain

F^{\prime\prime}(u,v,w)=\tilde{v}(e^{-u\cdot T}(u,v,w))=\tilde{v}\circ\exp_{T}(-u,(u,v,w)).

Thus
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
since
\exp_{T}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
.

Finally by (
3.18
) we have
F^{\prime\prime\prime}=G^{\prime\prime}
in a neighborhood of
0\in\mathfrak{M}
. Since
G=\Psi^{\mathrm{Inv}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
and
G^{\prime\prime}
is a component of
G
, we see that
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
.

By shrinking
U
if necessary, we conclude that
F^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
,
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
and
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
, finishing the proof of
(5)
and thus the whole proof.
∎

Remark 3.30
.

It would be natural to generalize the result to the multi-layers case, where we have a flag of involutive tangent subbundles
0\leq\mathcal{V}_{1}\leq\dots\leq\mathcal{V}_{k}\leq T\mathfrak{M}
. And the parameterization
\Phi(t^{(1)},\dots,t^{(k)},s)=e^{t^{(1)}T_{(1)}}\dots e^{t^{(k)}T_{(k)}}(\Gamma(s))
gives the parameterization with desired regularities, where
T_{(j)}
are collections of vector fields such that
T_{(1)},\dots,T_{(k)}
are linearly independent and
\mathcal{V}_{j}=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{R}}(T_{(1)},\dots,T_{(j)})
for each
j
. We will not use this generalization in the paper.

Using a similar but simpler construction from Theorem
3.27
, we can prove a holomorphic Frobenius theorem with parameter dependence. Here we endow
\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard (real and complex) coordinate system
\zeta=(\zeta^{1},\dots,\zeta^{n})
and
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
respectively.

Proposition 3.31

(Holomorphic Frobenius theorem with parameter)
.

Let
\tilde{U}_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{n}
and
V_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{q}
. Let
\beta>0
and
1\leq m\leq n
. And let
Z_{j}=\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{j}^{k}(\zeta,s)\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta^{k}}
,
j=1,\dots,m
be complex vector fields on
\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0}
such that

•

Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
are linearly independent and pairwise commutative at every point in
\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0}

•

b_{j}^{k}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0};\mathbb{C}^{n})
are holomorphic in
w
-variable.

Then for any
(\zeta_{0},s_{0})\in\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0}
there is a neighborhood
\tilde{U}\times V\subseteq\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0}
of
(\zeta_{0},s_{0})
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-map
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}:\tilde{U}\times V\to\mathbb{C}^{n-m}
, such that

(i)

\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta_{0},s_{0})=0
.

(ii)

\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\tilde{U},V;\mathbb{C}^{n-m})
is holomorphic in
\zeta
-variable. In particular
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{\zeta,s,\mathrm{loc}}
and
\nabla_{\zeta}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{\zeta,s,\mathrm{loc}}
.

(iii)

For each
s\in V
, the contangent subbundle
\operatorname{Span}(Z_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,Z_{m}(\cdot,s))^{\bot}|_{\tilde{U}}\leq\mathbb{C}T^{*}\tilde{U}
has a holomorphic local basis
(d\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime 1}(\cdot,s),\dots,d\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime n-m}(\cdot,s),d\bar{\zeta}^{1},\dots,d\bar{\zeta}^{n})
.

Let
U:=\tilde{U}\cap\mathbb{R}^{n}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be the open set in the real space endowed with the real coordinates
(\xi^{1},\dots,\xi^{n})
where
\xi=\operatorname{Re}\zeta
. Let
X_{j}:=\sum_{k=1}^{n}b_{j}^{k}(\xi+i0,s)\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi^{k}}
for
1\leq j\leq m
be the “real domain restriction” of
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
. Then

(iv)

For each
s\in V
, the cotangent subbundle
\operatorname{Span}(X_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,X_{m}(\cdot,s))^{\bot}\leq\mathbb{C}T^{*}U
has a real-analytic local basis
\big(d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime 1}(\cdot,s)|_{U}),\dots,d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime n-m}(\cdot,s)|_{U})\big)
.

Remark 3.32
.

We state
(iii)
and
(iv)
in this way because the pointwise span of
dG^{\prime\prime}
or
d(G^{\prime\prime}|_{U\times V})
does not make sense when
\beta\leq 1
.

When
\beta>1
, the result
(iv)
is the same as to say that the
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-subbundle
\operatorname{Span}(X_{1},\dots,X_{m})\leq\mathbb{C}T^{*}(U\times V)
has a local basis
(d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime 1}|_{U\times V}),\dots,d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime n-m}|_{U\times V}),ds^{1},\dots,ds^{q})
. Similar result holds for
(iii)
.

Proof of Proposition
3.31
.

Endow two complex spaces
\mathbb{C}^{m}
and
\mathbb{C}^{n-m}
with standard complex coordinate
z=(z^{1},\dots,z^{m})
and
w=(w^{1},\dots,w^{n-m})
.

Since
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
are linearly independent, the span of
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
is a rank
m
complex subbundle on
\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0}
. Hence we can find an affine complex linear map
\Gamma:\mathbb{C}^{n-m}_{w}\to\mathbb{C}^{n}_{\zeta}
such that
\Gamma(0)=\zeta_{0}
and

\partial_{w^{1}}\Gamma,\dots,\partial_{w^{n-m}}\Gamma,Z_{1}|_{(\zeta_{0},s_{0})},\dots,Z_{m}|_{(\zeta,s)}\in\mathbb{C}T_{\zeta_{0}}\tilde{U}_{0}\text{ are $\mathbb{C}$-linearly independent}.

(3.20)

We define
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-maps
\Psi(z,w,s)
to
\tilde{U}_{0}
and
\widehat{\Psi}(z,w,s)
to
\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0}
as

\Psi(z,w,s):=e^{z\cdot Z}(\Gamma(w),s+s_{0})=\big(e^{z^{1}Z_{1}(\cdot,s+s_{0})}\dots e^{z^{m}Z_{m}(\cdot,s+s_{0})}(\Gamma(w))\big),\quad\widehat{\Psi}(z,w,s):=(\Psi(z,w,s),s+s_{0}).

Here
e^{z^{j}Z_{j}(\cdot,s)}(\zeta)=\Phi^{Z_{j}}(z_{j},\zeta,s)
is given by (
3.8
) and (
3.10
).

Clearly
\Psi(0,0,0)=(\zeta_{0},s_{0})
. By Lemma
3.25

(i)
and taking compositions,
\Psi
is defined in a neighborhood
\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
of
0
, such that
\Psi\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\tilde{U}_{0})
and
\Psi
is holomorphic in
(z,w)
.

Since
\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{j}}e^{z^{j}Z_{j}}=Z_{j}\circ e^{z^{j}Z_{j}}
from (
3.8
), by Lemma
3.20
we have

\textstyle\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{j}}\widehat{\Psi}(z,w,s)=Z_{j}\circ\widehat{\Psi}(z,w,s),\quad 1\leq j\leq m,\quad(z,w,s)\in\Omega.

(3.21)

By condition (
3.20
) we see that
\nabla_{z,w}\Psi(\cdot,0)
is of full rank in a neighborhood of
0
.
Therefore, by Lemma
2.32

(iii)
and shrinking
\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}
if necessary,
\Psi(\cdot,s):\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\to\tilde{U}_{0}
is locally biholomorphic for each
s\in\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\widehat{\Psi}^{\mathrm{Inv}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\tilde{U},V;\mathbb{C}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{q})
for some neighborhood
\tilde{U}\times V\subseteq\tilde{U}_{0}\times V_{0}
of
(\zeta_{0},s_{0})
. Thus we get the well-defined map
\widetilde{G}(\zeta,s):=\Psi(\cdot,s-s_{0})^{\mathrm{Inv}}(\zeta)
for
(\zeta,s)\in\tilde{U}\times V
with
\widetilde{G}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{\zeta,s}
.

Write
\widetilde{G}=(\widetilde{G}^{\prime},\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime},\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime\prime})
with respect to the
z
,
w
,
s
-coordinate components. We are going to show that
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}:\tilde{U}\times V\to\mathbb{C}^{n-m}
is as desired.

Immediately
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta_{0},s_{0})=0
because
\widehat{\Psi}^{\mathrm{Inv}}(\zeta,s)=(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\zeta,s),s-s_{0})
and
\widehat{\Psi}(0)=(\zeta_{0},s_{0})
. We have
(i)
.

Since
\widetilde{G}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{\zeta,s}(\tilde{U},V;\mathbb{C}^{n})
and
\Psi
is holomorphic in
z,w
, we know
\widetilde{G}
is holomorphic in
\zeta
. By Lemma
2.19
we get
\widetilde{G}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\zeta}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
. In particular
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\zeta}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
is holomorphic in
\zeta
, giving
(ii)
.

To prove
(iii)
and
(iv)
we can fix a
s\in V
. Thus without loss of generality
V=\{\ast\}
is a singleton, and we can assume
Z(\zeta,s)=Z(\zeta)
and
\widetilde{G}(\zeta,s)=\widetilde{G}(\zeta)
in the following argument.

Proof of
(iii)
: Now
\widetilde{G}:\tilde{U}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
is a complex (holomorphic) coordinate chart, so
d\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime 1},\dots,d\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime n-m},d\bar{\zeta}^{1},\dots,d\bar{\zeta}^{n}
are linearly independent differentials on
\tilde{U}
.

By (
3.21
) we have
Z_{j}=\Psi_{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{j}}=\widetilde{G}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{j}}
, thus
Z_{j}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime k}=(\widetilde{G}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{j}})(\widetilde{G}^{*}dw^{k})=0
all vanish for
1\leq j\leq m
and
1\leq k\leq n-m
.
This finishes the proof of
(iii)
.

Proof of
(iv)
: By
(iii)
, we know the
n\times(n-m)
matrix
(\partial_{\zeta^{j}}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime k})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq n\\
1\leq k\leq n-m\end{subarray}}
has full rank
n-m
at every point in
\tilde{U}
.
By
(ii)

\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}
is holomorphic in
\zeta
, so
\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}=\partial_{\zeta}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}
. Therefore, the matrix-valued function
(\partial_{\xi^{j}}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime k})_{n\times(n-m)}:U\to\mathbb{C}^{n\times(n-m)}
still has rank
n-m
at every point. Since
d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime k}|_{U})=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime k}}{\partial\xi^{j}}d\xi^{j}
, we conclude that
d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}|_{U})
is a collection of
(n-m)
-differentials that are linearly independent on
U
.

Since
\partial_{\xi}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}=\partial_{\zeta}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}
and
Z_{j}\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}=0
from above, we know
X_{j}(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}|_{U})=0
as well, for
1\leq j\leq m
. Therefore,
d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}|_{U})
annihilates
X_{1},\dots,X_{m}
on
U
. This completes the proof of
(iv)
.
∎

4
Some Elliptic PDEs

In this section we will prove two results which occur in the key steps of Malgrange’s factorization.

4.1
Existence of a particular PDE

In this subsection we use
\tau=(\tau^{1},\dots,\tau^{r})
,
w=(w^{1},\dots,w^{m})
,
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
as the standard (real or complex) coordinate system for
\mathbb{R}^{r}
,
\mathbb{C}^{m}
and
\mathbb{R}^{q}
respectively. We consider the unit balls
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}
and
\mathbb{B}^{q}\subset\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
.

Convention 4.1
.

We denote
\mathcal{I}:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{B}^{2m}
,
\mathcal{I}(\tau,w,s):=w
as the projection map.

For a matrix
B=(b_{j}^{k})
, we use
b_{j}^{k}
to denote the
j
-th row
k
-th column entry of
B
.

We use
A=\begin{pmatrix}A^{\prime}\\
A^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}
as a matrix map,
H^{\prime\prime}=(H^{\prime\prime 1},\dots,H^{\prime\prime m}):\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
as a vector-valued map whose image is set to be a
m
-dimensional row vector. We use

H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime 1}}{\partial\tau^{1}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime m}}{\partial\tau^{1}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime 1}}{\partial\tau^{r}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime m}}{\partial\tau^{r}}\end{pmatrix},\quad H^{\prime\prime}_{w}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime 1}}{\partial w^{1}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime m}}{\partial w^{1}}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime 1}}{\partial w^{m}}&\cdots&\frac{\partial H^{\prime\prime m}}{\partial w^{m}}\end{pmatrix},\quad\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}=\begin{pmatrix}H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
H^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}

(4.1)

Let
\Psi,\Theta,\Lambda
be matrix-valued functions of
H^{\prime\prime}
and
A
as follow:

\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}]:=\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix},\ \Theta[A;H^{\prime\prime}]:=\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix},\ \Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]:=\Theta[A;H^{\prime\prime}]^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}.

(4.2)

Remark 4.2
.

One can see that
\Psi,\Theta,\Lambda
are all rational functions in the components of
A
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}
.

We are going to consider the following PDE system, whose deduction is in Lemma
5.6
: for
l=1,\dots,m
,
(\tau,w)\in\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}
and
s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{j}\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{j}^{l}\Bigg|_{(\tau,w;s)}

(4.3)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{k+r+m}\!\!\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{k+r}^{l}\Bigg|_{(\tau,w;s)}=0.

Recall
\partial_{\tau}=[\partial_{\tau^{1}},\dots,\partial_{\tau^{r}}]^{\top}
in Section
1.4
and the space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
in Definition
2.6
. In this subsection we prove the following:

Proposition 4.3
.

Using Convention
4.1
, for any
\alpha\in(1,\infty)
,
\beta\in(0,\alpha+1]
and
\varepsilon>0
, there is a
\delta=\delta(\varepsilon,\alpha,\beta,r,m,q)>0
, such that if
A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
satisfies
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}}<\delta
, then there is a
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that

(i)

H^{\prime\prime}
solves the following PDE system (
4.3
) and satisfies the boundary condition
H^{\prime\prime}|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\mathcal{I}
.

(ii)

Let
H(\tau,w,s):=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
. Then
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
is homeomorphism, and
H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

(iii)

Endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}
and
\mathbb{C}^{m}
with another (real or complex) coordinate system
\sigma=(\sigma^{1},\dots,\sigma^{r})
and
\zeta=(\zeta^{1},\dots,\zeta^{m})
. Let
\tilde{\Phi}:=H^{\mathrm{Inv}}:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
be the inverse map. Then
\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\circ\tilde{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\sigma,\zeta}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\sigma,\zeta}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
. Moreover

\|\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\circ\tilde{\Phi}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}<\varepsilon.

(4.4)

As an immediate corollary to Proposition
4.3
we have:

Corollary 4.4
.

The map
H^{\prime\prime}
in the consequence of Proposition
4.3
satisfies
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
.

Proof.

By assumption
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
.
Remark
2.13
we have
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}
, thus
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
.
By Remark
2.7

(vi)
we have
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\subset\mathscr{C}^{\varepsilon}\mathscr{C}^{\beta-\varepsilon}\subset L^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta-\varepsilon}
for every
0<\varepsilon<\alpha
. Thus
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap(\bigcap_{0<\varepsilon<\beta}L^{\infty}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta-\varepsilon}_{s})=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
.
∎

Before we prove the Proposition
4.3
, we need to show the PDE (
4.3
) is well-defined, in the sense that
\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}](\tau,w,s)
and
\Theta[A;H^{\prime\prime}](\tau,w,s)
are invertible matrices for all
(\tau,w,s)\in\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

For
\alpha,\beta>0
and
\varepsilon>0
, we define spaces

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon}:=\{A\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}):\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}}<\varepsilon\},

(4.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon}:=\{H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):H^{\prime\prime}|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}\equiv\mathcal{I},\ \|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}<\varepsilon\},

with metrics induced by the norm structures of their respective ambient Banach spaces.

Lemma 4.5
.

For any
\alpha>1
and
\beta>0
there is a
\varepsilon_{1}=\varepsilon_{1}(r,m,q,\alpha,\beta)>0
such that

(i)

For every
A\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}
,
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}
,
(\tau,w)\in\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}
and
s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
, the matrices
\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}](\tau,w,s)
and
\Theta[A;H^{\prime\prime}](\tau,w,s)
given in (
4.2
) are both invertible.

(ii)

For every
A\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}
and
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}
,

\|\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}\leq\varepsilon_{1}^{-1}(\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}+\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}}).

(4.6)

(iii)

Denote by
T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]^{l}
the left hand side of (
4.3
) for
l=1,\dots,m
, and
T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]:=(T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]^{l})_{l=1}^{m}
as the vector-valued function. Then
T:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
is Fréchet differentiable. Moreover for every
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}
,

\|T[0;H^{\prime\prime}]-(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\leq\varepsilon_{1}^{-1}\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}^{2}.

(4.7)

Here
\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w}=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\tau^{j}\partial\tau^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial w^{k}\partial\bar{w}^{k}}
is the mixed real and complex Laplacian.

Proof.

We let
\varepsilon_{1}
be a small constant which may change from line to line.

Note that when
H^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{I}
and
A=0
, we have

\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}=\begin{pmatrix}0_{r\times m}\\
I_{m}\\
0_{m\times m}\end{pmatrix},\quad\Psi[\mathcal{I}]=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}\\
&I_{m}\\
&&I_{m}\end{pmatrix}=I_{r+2m},\quad\Theta[0;\mathcal{I}]=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}+0_{r\times r}&0_{r\times m}\\
0_{m\times r}&I_{m}+0_{m\times m}\end{pmatrix}=I_{r+m}.

By cofactor representations of the matrices,
\Psi^{-1}
,
\Theta^{-1}
and
\Lambda
are rational functions in the components of
A
,
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}
, which can be expressed as power series expansions in the components of
A
,
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}
at the point
A=0
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}=\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}=\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}
.

By Corollary
2.21

(ii)

\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
is closed under products. By Corollary
2.22
, inverting a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
-matrix is a real-analytic map near the identity matrix. Therefore there is a
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}>0
such that if
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}<\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}
and
\|\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}<\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}
, then
\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}]
and
\Theta[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
are invertible matrices at every point in
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
, and moreover as maps between
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
-spaces,

\displaystyle\Psi^{-1}:\{\|\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}<\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}\}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s},

\displaystyle\Lambda:\{\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}<\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}\}\times\{\|\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}<\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}\}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s},

are both real-analytic.

Clearly
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}\hookrightarrow\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
(see Remark
2.13
) and
\nabla_{\tau,w}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
. Thus there is a
\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}<C^{-1}\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}
where
C=C(\alpha,\beta,r,m,q)>0
is a large constant, such that

A\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}},\ H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}+\|\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}<\tilde{\varepsilon}_{1}.

(4.8)

Therefore when
\varepsilon\leq\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}
we see that
\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}]
and
\Theta[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
are pointwise invertible in
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
, finishing the proof of
(i)
. Moreover we have the following Fréchet differentiable maps (which are in fact real-analytic):

\displaystyle\Psi^{-1}:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times(r+2m)}),

(4.9)

\displaystyle\Lambda:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m}).

Since
\Psi[\mathcal{I}]=I_{r+2m}
and
\Lambda[0;\mathcal{I}]=0
, taking the first order terms of the power expansion we have

\displaystyle\|\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}]^{-1}-I_{r+2m}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}-\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}},

(4.10)

\displaystyle\|\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}\lesssim_{\alpha}\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}+\|\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}-\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}},

(4.11)

whenever
A\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}
and
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\tilde{\varepsilon}_{2}}
. Therefore by (
4.11
) with possibly shrinking
\varepsilon_{1}
, we get (
4.6
) and prove
(ii)
.

We now prove
(iii)
. From the expression (
4.3
),
T[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
is the linear combinations to the components of
\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}]^{-1}\otimes\nabla_{\tau,w}\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]
. As the maps in (
4.9
),
\Psi^{-1}
and
\Lambda
are both Fréchet differentiable. Therefore

\big[(A,H^{\prime\prime})\mapsto\nabla_{\tau,w}\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\big]:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m\times(r+2m)}),

is differentiable.

By Corollary
2.21

(i)
the product map
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}\times\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
is bounded hence is differentiable, we see that

\big[(A,H^{\prime\prime})\mapsto\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}]^{-1}\otimes\nabla_{\tau,w}\Lambda[A;H^{\prime\prime}]\big]:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)^{2}\times m(r+m)(r+2m)}),

is also differentiable. Taking linear combinations we get that
T:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
is Fréchet differentiable.

Since
H^{\prime\prime}\mapsto\Lambda[0;H^{\prime\prime}]
can a real-analytic function to the components of
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}
, taking second order power expansion at
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}=\nabla_{\tau,w}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}=\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}
we have

\Lambda[0;H^{\prime\prime}]=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&\\
&I_{m}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\end{pmatrix}+O(|\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}-\nabla_{\tau,w}\mathcal{I}|^{2}).

By Corollary
2.21

(i)
again, since
\varepsilon_{1}
is small, we have

\bigg\|\Lambda[0;H^{\prime\prime}]-\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\end{pmatrix}\bigg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}\lesssim\|\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{0}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}^{2}\lesssim\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}^{2}.

(4.12)

Taking
\nabla_{\tau,w}
on
\Lambda[0;H^{\prime\prime}]
, since by (
4.10
) that
\Psi[H^{\prime\prime}]^{-1}=I_{r+2m}+O(|\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})|)
near
H^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{I}
, we have

\displaystyle T[0;H^{\prime\prime}]^{l}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{r}\left((I_{r+2m}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{j}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\end{pmatrix}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))^{2}\right)_{j}^{l}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\left((I_{r+2m}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{k+r+m}\left(\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\end{pmatrix}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))^{2}\right)_{k+r}^{l}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau^{j}}\frac{\partial\bar{H}^{\prime\prime l}}{\partial\tau^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{k}}\frac{\partial\bar{H}^{\prime\prime l}}{\partial\bar{w}^{k}}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))^{2}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))O(\nabla^{2}_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))^{2}+O(\nabla_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}))O(\nabla^{2}_{\tau,w}(H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I})).

Combining (
4.10
), (
4.11
), (
4.12
) and taking
\varepsilon_{1}
smaller, we therefore get (
4.7
), finishing the proof of
(iii)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.3
.

By Lemma
4.5

(iii)
,
T:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\times\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q},\mathbb{C}^{m})
is a well-defined Fréchet differentiable map for some small
\varepsilon_{1}>0
. By (
4.7
), since
T[0;\mathcal{I}]=0
, we know the tangential map of
T
with respect to
H^{\prime\prime}
at
(A,H^{\prime\prime})=(0,\mathcal{I})
is

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial T}{\partial H^{\prime\prime}}\right|_{A=0,H^{\prime\prime}=\mathcal{I}}=(\Delta_{\tau}+\Box_{w})\overline{(\cdot)}:

\displaystyle\{F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):F|_{(\partial\mathbb{B}^{r+2m})\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=0\}

(4.13)

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}).

By Lemma
2.31
(passing to a scaling on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}
), we see that the operator (
4.13
) is invertible. Therefore by the standard Implicit Function Theorem on Banach space, we can find a
\delta_{1}
and a continuous map
\mathfrak{h}:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\delta_{1}}\to\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon_{1}}
such that
T[A;\mathfrak{h}[A]]=0
holds for all
A\in\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\delta_{1}}
. By continuity of
\mathfrak{h}
, we see that for any
\varepsilon>0
there is a
\delta>0
such that
\mathfrak{h}:\mathcal{U}_{\alpha,\beta,\delta}\to\mathcal{V}_{\alpha,\beta,\varepsilon}
holds. Taking
H^{\prime\prime}=\mathfrak{h}[A]
we can finish the proof of
(i)
.

The results
(ii)
and
(iii)
are the direct consequence to Lemma
2.30
and Proposition
2.27
by considering
F((\tau,w),s):=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s))
. Note that
H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
is automatically satisfied if
\|H^{\prime\prime}-\mathcal{I}\|_{C^{0}}<\frac{1}{4}
, which can be obtained whenever
\delta_{0}
is suitably small.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

By Lemma
4.5
, one can see that no matter how large
\beta>0
is (in particular for
\beta>\alpha+1
), we can find a
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(\alpha,\beta,r,m,q)>0
such that: if
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}}<\delta_{0}
then there is a
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
satisfying results
(i)
and
(ii)
in Proposition
4.3
. We only consider the case
\beta\leq\alpha+1
because for the inverse map
\tilde{\Phi}=H^{\mathrm{Inv}}
we only have
\tilde{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\min(\alpha+1,\beta)}_{(\zeta,\sigma),s}
, where the regularity
\mathscr{C}^{\min(\alpha+1,\beta)}
for parameters is optimal.

4.2
Real-analyticity of a particular PDE

In this part on a complex space we denote the fixed open cone

\mathbb{H}^{n}=\{x+iy:x,y\in\mathbb{B}^{n},\ 4|y|<1-|x|\}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}_{z}.

(4.14)

Clearly
\mathbb{H}^{n}_{z}\cap\mathbb{R}^{n}_{x}=\mathbb{B}^{n}
is a “complex extension” of the unit ball
\mathbb{B}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
in the real domain.

For a holomorphic function
f\in\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
, we denote
\partial f=\partial_{z}f=(\frac{\partial f}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial f}{\partial z^{n}})
as a
n
-dimensional vector valued function.

In this part we give the proof of the following regularity result for a quasilinear elliptic equation system:

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
m,n,p,q\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, let
\Theta:\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{C}^{n\times m}\to\mathbb{C}^{p}
be a complex bilinear map, and let
L=\sum_{j=1}^{n}a^{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{j}}
be a vector-valued first order differential operator on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with constant coefficients
a^{j}\in\mathbb{C}^{m\times p}
.

Assume
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
,
\beta>0
, there is a
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(m,n,p,q,\alpha,\beta,\Theta,L)>0
that satisfies the following:

Suppose
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{x}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{x}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
satisfies
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{x}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{x}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}}<\varepsilon_{0}
and
\Delta_{x}f=L_{x}\Theta(f,\partial_{x}f)
, then
f
admits extension
\mathfrak{f}:\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
such that
\mathfrak{f}
is holomorphic in
z
and
\mathfrak{f}(x+i0;s)=f(x;s)
for
x\in\mathbb{B}^{n},s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
.
Moreover
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

The proof of Proposition
4.7
is based on the following Holomorphic extensibility for Laplacian.

Proposition 4.8

(Holormorphic property of Laplacian)
.

Let
\mathbb{B}^{n}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathbb{H}^{n}\subset\mathbb{C}^{n}
be as above.

(i)

There is a linear operator
\mathbf{P}
acting on functions on
\mathbb{B}^{n}
such that
\Delta\mathbf{P}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}
and
\mathbf{P}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
is bounded for all
-2<\alpha<1
.

(ii)

There is a linear operator
\tilde{\mathbf{P}}
acting on functions on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
, such that for every
-2<\alpha<1
:

•

\tilde{\mathbf{P}}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
is bounded linear;

•

\Delta\tilde{\mathbf{P}}u=u
and
(\tilde{\mathbf{P}}u)|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}}=\mathbf{P}(u|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}})
for all
u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
. Here
\Delta=\Delta_{x}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial x^{j}\partial x^{j}}
.

(iii)

If
\Delta u=0
in
\mathbb{B}^{n}
, then
u
extend holomorphically into
\mathbb{H}^{n}
, call it
\mathbf{E}u\in\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
. Moreover the holomorphic extension operator
\mathbf{E}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n};\mathbb{C})\cap\ker\Delta\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
is bounded for all
-2<\alpha<1
.

Remark 4.9
.

Proposition
4.8
is essentially done in
[
Mor58
]
. However in our cases we need
\alpha
to be negative. So we include a complete proof in Section
B
.

Remark 4.10
.

In
(ii)
since
u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}
is holomorphic, the statement
\Delta_{x}\tilde{\mathbf{P}}u=u
is equivalent as
\Delta_{z}\tilde{\mathbf{P}}u=u
where
\Delta_{z}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z^{j}\partial z^{j}}
. In this case
\Delta_{z}
is NOT an elliptic operator on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
: it is not the complex Laplacian
\square=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z^{j}\partial\bar{z}^{j}}
.

We postpone the proof to Appendix
B
. Here the space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
is given by the following:

Definition 4.11
.

Let
\alpha<1
, we denote
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n};\mathbb{C})
as the space of holomorphic function
g\in\mathcal{O}(\mathbb{H})
such that

\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}}:=|g(0)|+\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb{H}^{n}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{1-\alpha}|\nabla g(z)|<\infty.

Let
\beta>0
and let
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\}
.
We define
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})=\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})
as the set of continuous function
f:\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}\to\mathbb{C}
such that

\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}:=\|f(0,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})}+\sup\limits_{z\in\mathbb{H}^{n}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{1-\alpha}\|\partial_{z}f(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})}<\infty.

(4.15)

Also we define the space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-\eta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
with norm
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-\eta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}:=\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}+\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-\eta}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}
, for
\eta<1
.

Remark 4.12
.

\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}(\mathbb{H}^{n})
is exactly the space of
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-functions on
\mathbb{H}^{n}
that are holomorphic in the interior. For part of the inclusion see Lemma
B.1
.

Remark 4.13
.

By the same argument to Lemma
2.9
, we see that
\mathbf{P},\tilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{E}
in Proposition
4.8
have natural extension to the parameter case. We still denote them as
\mathbf{P},\tilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{E}
: let
V\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{q}
be a bounded smooth subset, we have boundedness

•

\mathbf{P}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},V)\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},V)
for
-2<\alpha<1
and
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}:\beta\in\mathbb{R}\}
.

•

\tilde{\mathbf{P}}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},V)\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},V)
for
-2<\alpha<1
and
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}:\beta\in\mathbb{R}\}
.

•

\mathbf{E}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},V)\cap\Delta_{x}\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},V)
for
-2<\alpha<1
and
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}:\beta\in\mathbb{R}\}
.

We omit the proof to readers.

The following lemma is useful in the proof of Proposition
4.7
.

Lemma 4.14
.

Let
\beta>0
and let
\mathscr{X}\in\{L^{\infty},\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\}
.

(i)

The differentiation
[\tilde{f}\mapsto\partial_{z}\tilde{f}]:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{n})
is bounded linear for all
\alpha<1
.

(ii)

For
\alpha<0
,
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
has an equivalent norm

\textstyle\tilde{f}\mapsto\sup_{z\in\mathbb{H}^{n}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-\alpha}\|\tilde{f}(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})}.

(4.16)

(iii)

The restriction map
[\tilde{f}\mapsto\tilde{f}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}]:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{x}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})
is bounded linear for
0<\alpha<1
.

(iv)

We have inclusion map
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}\mathscr{X}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C})
for
0<\alpha<1
.

Proof.

These results follow from Lemma
B.1
, the parameter free cases, along with the same argument to Lemma
2.9
.
∎

We need to show that in
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
,
\mathfrak{f}\mapsto\Theta(\mathfrak{f},\partial_{z}\mathfrak{f})
maps
\mathfrak{f}
into the desired function space.

Lemma 4.15
.

Let
0<\alpha<1
and
\beta>0
.
For
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
and
g\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
, we have
fg\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})
. Moreover there is a
C=C(n,q,\alpha,\beta)>0
that does not depend on
f,g
such that

\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}\leq C\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q})}.

Proof.

Clearly the product
f(z,s)g(z,s)
is locally continuous and holomorphic in
z
.

By Lemma
4.14

(ii)
and
(iv)
, we have almost every
s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}
,

\displaystyle|(fg)(z,s)|\lesssim_{n,\alpha}\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\lesssim_{n,\alpha}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}.

Dividing
\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}
on both side, taking essential sup over
z
and
s
, and using Lemma
4.14

(ii)
we get the first control
\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
.

To control
\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
. By Lemmas
2.26

(ii)
and
4.14

(ii)
, for every
z\in\mathbb{H}^{n}
,

\displaystyle\|fg(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{q})}\lesssim_{\beta}\|f(z,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+\|f(z,\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g(z,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-2}+\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-1}\cdot\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{\alpha-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-2}.

Dividing
\operatorname{dist}(z,\partial\mathbb{H}^{n})^{-2}
on both side, we get
\|fg\|_{\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\|g\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
, completing the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.7
.

It suffices to consider
\frac{1}{2}<\alpha<1
.

We use
\Theta[u]=\Theta(u,\nabla_{x}u)
for function
u
either on
\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
or on
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
. Note that for
\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
we have
\nabla_{x}\tilde{u}=\partial_{z}\tilde{u}
.

The proof uses contraction mappings twice. The first one take place in
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and shows that
\mathbf{P}\Delta f
has some particular properties. The second one take place in
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, showing that
\mathbf{P}\Delta f
is a restriction of a function in
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
which is holomorphic in
z
.

For
u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, by Lemma
2.26
(since
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
) we have
u\otimes\nabla_{x}u(\cdot,s)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{x}
uniformly for almost every
s
, so
\Theta[u]\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{p})
and thus
L\Theta[u]\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Moreover we have for every
u,v\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[u]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Theta(u,\nabla_{x}u)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u\otimes\nabla_{x}u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}}^{2},

(4.17)

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[u]-L\Theta[v]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Theta(u-v,\nabla_{x}u)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}+\|\Theta(v,\nabla_{x}(u-v))\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}L^{\infty}}\lesssim(\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}})\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}.

Here the implicit constants depend only on
m,n,q,\alpha,L,\Theta
but not on
u
or
v
.

For
\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,
L\Theta[\tilde{u}]
is a function on
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
that is holormorphic in
z
. By Lemma
4.15
,
\tilde{u}\otimes\partial_{z}\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-2}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m^{2}n})
, we know
L\Theta[\tilde{u}]\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-3}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
. Moreover, similar to (
4.18
) we have for
\tilde{u},\tilde{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
:

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[\tilde{u}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-3}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}^{2},

(4.18)

\displaystyle\|L\Theta[\tilde{u}]-L\Theta[\tilde{v}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-3}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}})\|\tilde{u}-\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-1}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}.

Here the implicit constants depend only on
m,n,q,\alpha,\beta,L,\Theta
but not on
\tilde{u}
or
\tilde{v}
.

Let
\mathbf{P}:\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-2}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
be as in Proposition
4.8
. So the assumption
\Delta f=L\Theta[f]
implies that

f=f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f+\mathbf{P}\Delta f=(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\mathbf{P}L\Theta[\mathbf{P}\Delta f+(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)].

(4.19)

Define
T_{f}
on
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
as

T_{f}[u]:=(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\mathbf{P}L\Theta[u+(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)],\quad u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}).

Since
\|f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}
. By (
4.17
) and Remark
4.13
, we can find a
C_{1}=C_{1}(m,n,q,\alpha,L,\Theta,\mathbf{P})>1
that does not depend on
f
, that bounds (
4.17
) and satisfies
C_{1}>\|\operatorname{id}-\mathbf{P}\Delta\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}
. More precisely, for every
f,u,v\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q},\mathbb{C}^{m})
, we have

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+C_{1}\big(\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\big)^{2};

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]-T_{f}[v]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\left(\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}+C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\right)\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}};

\displaystyle\|f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}.

Take
\varepsilon_{1}>0
such that
9\varepsilon_{1}C_{1}+10\varepsilon_{1}C_{1}^{2}<1
. If
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{1}
and
\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}},\|v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
, then

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}+C_{1}(3C_{1}\varepsilon_{1})^{2}<2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1},

\displaystyle\|T_{f}[u]-T_{f}[v]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<C_{1}(5C_{1}\varepsilon_{1})\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\tfrac{1}{2}\|u-v\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}.

Therefore when
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{1}
,
T_{f}
is a contraction mapping in
\{u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}\}
. In such case, there is a unique
u
satisfying
\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
such that
T_{f}[u]=u
. On the other hand,
f\in\{u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\|u\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}\}
satisfies (
4.19
). Thus
f
is that unique fixed point.

Similarly, by Proposition
4.8

(i)
(also see Remark
4.13
), we have boundedness
\mathbf{P}:\mathscr{C}^{-3}(\mathbb{B}^{n})\to\mathscr{C}^{-1}(\mathbb{B}^{n})
, so
\|f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}
. Note that
f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f
is harmonic function in
x
-variable, so by Proposition
4.8

(iii)
we have
\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
with

\|\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\lesssim_{\alpha,\beta}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}.

(4.20)

For a
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, define
\tilde{T}_{f}
on
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
as

\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]=\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\tilde{\mathbf{P}}L\Theta[\tilde{u}+\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)],\quad\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}).

Here
\tilde{\mathbf{P}}
is as in Proposition
4.8

(ii)
.

Take
C_{2}=C_{2}(m,n,q,\alpha,\beta,L,\Theta,\tilde{\mathbf{P}},\mathbf{E})>1
that bounds (
4.18
) and (
4.20
). Therefore, for every
\tilde{u},\tilde{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
,

\displaystyle\|\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+C_{2}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+C_{2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\big)^{2};

\displaystyle\|\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]-\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{v}]\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}+C_{2}\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\big)\|\tilde{u}-\tilde{v}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}.

By Lemma
4.14

(iii)
we can find a
C_{3}=C_{3}(m,n,q,\alpha,\beta)>0
such that,

\|\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\leq C_{3}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})},\quad\forall u\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}).

(4.21)

Take
\varepsilon_{0}>0
such that
C_{3}C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}
and
9\varepsilon_{0}C_{2}+10\varepsilon_{0}C_{2}^{2}<1
. So
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}<\varepsilon_{0}
implies
\|f\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{1}
.
For such
f
, we know
\tilde{T}_{f}
is a contraction mapping in
\{\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m}):\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}}\leq 2C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}\}
.
Thus there is a unique
\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\|\tilde{u}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathcal{O}}}\leq 2C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}
and
\tilde{u}=\tilde{T}_{f}[\tilde{u}]
.

By Proposition
4.8

(ii)
,

\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=\big(\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)+\tilde{\mathbf{P}}L\Theta[\tilde{u}+\mathbf{E}(f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f)]\big)\big|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}=f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f+\mathbf{P}L\Theta[\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}+f-\mathbf{P}\Delta f]=T_{f}[\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}].

By (
4.21
) and the assumption
C_{3}C_{2}\varepsilon_{0}<\varepsilon_{1}<2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
, we have
\|\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}(\mathbb{B}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})}\leq 2C_{1}\varepsilon_{1}
. By the uniqueness of the fixed point for
T_{f}
, we get
f=\tilde{u}|_{\mathbb{B}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}}
.

So
\mathfrak{f}=\tilde{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{-1}_{\mathcal{O}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
is the desired extension of
f
to
\mathbb{H}^{n}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

Finally, by Lemma
2.19
we have
\mathfrak{f}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{n},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
, completing the proof.
∎

5
Malgrange’s Factorization with Parameters

In this section we prove the key part of Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{N}
be two smooth manifolds. In this section we consider the product manifold
\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}
as the ambient space, so
(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}=\coprod_{q\in\mathfrak{N}}\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M}
is a subbundle of
\mathbb{C}T(\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N})
whose (complex) rank equals to the (real) dimension of
\mathfrak{M}
.

We consider a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}
involutive complex tangential subbundle
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}
such that
\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
and
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}=(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
. Note that
(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
is automatically involutive, so
\mathcal{S}
is a complex Frobenius structure.

We recall the space
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M},\mathfrak{N})
(
\alpha>1
,
\beta>0
) in Definition
3.2
, the
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}
-subbundle
\mathcal{S}
in Definition
3.6
, and the involutivity of
\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
in Definition
3.7
(also see Remark
3.8
). We endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}
,
\mathbb{C}^{m}
and
\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard (real and complex) coordinate system
t=(t^{1},\dots,t^{r})
,
z=(z^{1},\dots,z^{m})
and
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
respectively.

Theorem 5.1

(The key estimate)
.

Let
r,m,q\geq 0
, let
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathfrak{N}
be two smooth manifolds with dimensions
(r+2m)
and
q
respectively. Let
\alpha\in(1,\infty)
and
\beta\in(0,\alpha+1]
. Let
\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
be a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}
-involutive subbundle of rank
r+m
such that
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}=(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
.

Then for any
u_{0}\in\mathfrak{M}
and
v_{0}\in\mathfrak{N}
, there are neighborhoods
U\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
of
u_{0}
,
V\subseteq\mathfrak{N}
of
v_{0}
and a map
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):U\times V\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
such that

(1)

F^{\prime\prime\prime}:V\to\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
is a smooth coordinate chart of
\mathfrak{N}
that is independent of
U\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
, such that
F^{\prime\prime\prime}(v_{0})=0
;
F^{\prime}:U\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}
is a smooth map that is independent of
V\subseteq\mathfrak{N}
, such that
F^{\prime}(u_{0})=0
.

(2)

F:U\times V\to F(U\times V)
is homeomorphism. And for each
v\in V
,
[u\mapsto(F^{\prime}(u),F^{\prime\prime}(u,v))]:U\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-coordinate chart.

(3)

F^{\prime\prime}(u_{0},v_{0})=0
,
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U,V;\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
XF^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U,V;\mathbb{C}^{m})
for every
C^{\infty}
vector field
X
on
\mathfrak{M}
.

(4)

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{m}}
are well-defined
\mathscr{C}^{\min(\alpha,\beta-)}
-vector fields and span
\mathcal{S}|_{U\times V}
.

For the parameterization side, let
\Phi=(\Phi^{\prime},\Phi^{\prime\prime}):F(U\times V)\to\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}
be the inverse map of
F
, and let
\Omega:=\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
be any neighborhood of
(0,0,0)
which is contained in
F(U\times V)
. Then

(5)

\Phi^{\prime\prime}:V\to\mathfrak{N}
is a smooth regular parameterization. Moreover
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=(F^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{Inv}}
.

(6)

\Phi:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}
is homeomorphic onto its image, and
\Phi(0,0,0)=(u_{0},v_{0})
. For each
s\in\Omega^{\prime\prime}
,
\Phi^{\prime}(\cdot,s):\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\to\mathfrak{M}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-regular parameterization.

(7)

\Phi^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathfrak{M})
and
\frac{\partial\Phi^{\prime}}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial\Phi^{\prime}}{\partial t^{r}},\frac{\partial\Phi^{\prime}}{\partial z^{1}},\frac{\partial\Phi^{\prime}}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial\Phi^{\prime}}{\partial z^{m}},\frac{\partial\Phi^{\prime}}{\partial\bar{z}^{m}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})
.

(8)

\mathcal{S}_{\Phi(t,z,s)}\leq\mathbb{C}T_{\Phi^{\prime}(t,z,s)}\mathfrak{M}
is spanned by
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{1}}(t,z,s),\dots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{r}}(t,z,s),\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{1}}(t,z,s),\dots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{m}}(t,z,s)
,
\forall(t,z,s)\in\Omega
.

In particular,

(9)

When
1<\beta\leq\alpha+1
,
F
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-coordinate chart and
\Phi
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-regular parameterization.

Remark 5.2
.

(i)

By taking some modifications in the proof using
[
Str20
, Proposition B.4]
, Theorem
5.1
are also true for the case
\alpha=\infty
and
0<\beta<\infty
(cf.
[
Gon20
, Theorem 1.3(i)]
), we omit the detail to readers.

(ii)

We shall see in Section
7.2
that the index “
\beta-
” for
XF^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\in\mathscr{C}^{\min(\alpha,\beta-)}
are both sharp.

(iii)

Theorem
5.1
implies Street’s result
[
Str20
, Theorem 1.1]
for the sharp estimate on elliptic structure by considering
\alpha=\beta
and forgetting the
s
-parameter. (Recall
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\alpha}(U,V)=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(U\times V)
from Lemma
2.12
.)

Remark 5.3
.

Our results allow the range
\beta\in(0,1]
and
\beta\in(\alpha,\alpha+1]
.

For the case
\beta\leq 1
, it does not make sense to talk about
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-coordinate charts nor
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-parameterizations. Nevertheless by
(2)
we have

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}|_{(u,v)}=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,v))^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}|_{u}
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}|_{(u,v)}=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,v))^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}|_{u}
, since
(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,v))
is a coordinate chart for a fix
v\in V
. Alternatively by
(5)

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t}\circ F
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t}\circ F
are both continuous. Either way
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
are collections of pointwise defined vector fields.

For the case
\beta>\alpha
, the parameter part is actually more regular than the part of spatial variable. In this case
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
are
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-vector fields on
U\times V\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}
. See also Remark
4.6
.

Remark 5.4
.

If we only consider a family of elliptic structures rather than a family of complex Frobenius structures, and assume
\alpha,\beta
are non-integers, then Theorem
5.1
is much stronger than
[
Gon20
, Theorem 1.3]
:

•

We consider all
(\alpha,\beta)
such that
\alpha>1
and
0<\beta\leq\alpha+1
. In
[
Gon20
, Theorem 1.3]
only the case
\alpha>\beta+3\geq 4
and the case
\alpha=\beta
is considered.

•

For the regularity of the coordinate charts we have the sharp estimate
F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(U,V)
.
[
Gon20
, Theorem 1.3]
only gives
F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-,\beta-}(U,V)
.

We give a detailed overview to the idea of its proof in Section
5.1
using Malgrange’s factorization.

5.1
Overview of the key estimate

For vector fields
X_{1},\dots,X_{r+m}
, we write
X=[X_{1},\dots,X_{r+m}]^{\top}
following from the convention in Section
1.4
.

The most important part is to find the map
F^{\prime\prime}:U\times V\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
that has the desired regularity property. We factorize
F^{\prime\prime}
to the following composition of maps:

F^{\prime\prime}=\overline{G^{\prime\prime}}\circ H\circ L.

(5.1)

Endow
\mathbb{R}^{r},\mathbb{C}^{m},\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinates
\tau=(\tau^{1},\dots,\tau^{r})
,
w=(w^{1},\dots,w^{m})
and
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
respectively.
There are smooth coordinate charts
(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}):U_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}
and
L^{\prime\prime\prime}:V_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{q}\subset\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
, such that by taking
L=(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime},L^{\prime\prime\prime}):U_{0}\times V_{0}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
,
L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
-local basis on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}
with the form

X=\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
X^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&0&A^{\prime}\\
0&I_{m}&A^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix},\quad A:=\begin{pmatrix}A^{\prime}\\
A^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix},

(5.2)

where
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}
is small enough. This can be done by some scaling argument, see Lemma
5.5
.

There is a “
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
-coordinate change”
(\sigma,\zeta,s)=H(\tau,w,s)
where
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
is of the form
H(\tau,w,s)=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
, such that the local generators for
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
are
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
and real-analytic in
(\sigma,\zeta)
-variable. More precisely, the canonical local basis of
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
has the the form

\begin{pmatrix}T\\
Z\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&0&B^{\prime}\\
0&I_{m}&B^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\sigma}\\
\partial_{\zeta}\\
\partial_{\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix},\ B=\left.\begin{pmatrix}B^{\prime}\\
B^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right|_{(\sigma,\zeta,s)}:=\left.\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right|_{H^{\mathrm{Inv}}(\sigma,\zeta,s)}.

(5.3)

Here
B^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime}
are defined on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\subset(\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m})\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
and admit
(\sigma,\zeta)
-variables holomorphic extensions to the domain
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}\subset\mathbb{C}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
.
In this case we note that
H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau},H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
has a regularity loss on the parameter, while
T,Z\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{(\sigma,\zeta),s}
retrieve the full
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
regularity on the parameter.

We denote by
\mathfrak{T}
and
\mathfrak{Z}
the collections of holomorphic vector fields corresponding to
T
and
Z
. Finally, applying Proposition
3.31
, the holomorphic Frobenius theorem with parameter, on
\mathfrak{T},\mathfrak{Z}
we can find a
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\sigma,\zeta}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
-map
G^{\prime\prime}(\sigma,\zeta,s)
such that
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}^{\bot}
is spanned by
dG^{\prime\prime},d\bar{\zeta},ds
. See Lemma
5.8
.

In this way, the composition map
\overline{G^{\prime\prime}}\circ H\circ L
is as desired.

To make
B(\sigma,\zeta,s)
real-analytic, we impose extra conditions on
H
: We require
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}_{\tau,w}L^{\infty}_{s}\cap\mathscr{C}^{1}_{\tau,w}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{s}
, and
H
satisfies the following condition:

\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial T_{j}}{\partial\sigma^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial Z_{k}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}=0.

(5.4)

Rewriting (
5.4
) in the
(\tau,w,s)
-space using the coordinate change
H
, we get (
4.3
). See Lemma
5.6
for this deduction. The existence of such
H
is done in Proposition
4.3
.

We write
B^{\prime}=(b_{j}^{l})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r\\
1\leq l\leq m\end{subarray}}
and
B^{\prime\prime}=(b_{r+k}^{l})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k\leq m\\
1\leq l\leq m\end{subarray}}
.
By Lemma
3.9

(ii)
the involutivity of
\mathcal{S}
implies that
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
are commutative. Writing out the conditions
[T_{j},T_{j^{\prime}}]=0
,
[T_{j},Z_{k}]=0
and
[Z_{k},Z_{k^{\prime}}]=0
in terms of
B^{\prime}
and
B^{\prime\prime}
and combining (
5.4
), we have the following:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial b_{j^{\prime}}^{l}}{\partial\sigma^{j}}-\frac{\partial b_{j}^{l}}{\partial\sigma^{j^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{m}\Big(b_{j^{\prime}}^{q}\frac{\partial b_{j}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{q}}-b_{j}^{q}\frac{\partial b_{j^{\prime}}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{q}}\Big),

\displaystyle 1\leq j<j^{\prime}\leq r,1\leq l\leq m;

(5.5)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial b_{r+k}^{l}}{\partial\sigma^{j}}-\frac{\partial b_{j}^{l}}{\partial\zeta^{k}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{m}\Big(b_{r+k}^{q}\frac{\partial b_{j}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{q}}-b_{j}^{q}\frac{\partial b_{r+k}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{q}}\Big),

\displaystyle 1\leq j\leq r,1\leq k,l\leq m;

\displaystyle\frac{\partial b_{r+k^{\prime}}^{l}}{\partial\zeta^{k}}-\frac{\partial b_{r+k}^{l}}{\partial\zeta^{k^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{q=1}^{m}\Big(b_{r+k^{\prime}}^{q}\frac{\partial b_{r+k}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{q}}-b_{r+k}^{q}\frac{\partial b_{r+k^{\prime}}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{q}}\Big),

\displaystyle 1\leq k<{k^{\prime}}\leq m,1\leq l\leq m;

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial b_{j}^{l}}{\partial\sigma^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial b_{r+k}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle 1\leq l\leq m.

(
5.5
) is a quasilinear PDE system of the form
DB=\Theta(B,\nabla_{\sigma,\zeta}B)
, where
D
