is a first order differential operator with constant (vector-valued) coefficients and
\Theta
is a product map with constant (vector-valued) coefficients. We can check that
D^{*}D=\Delta_{\sigma}+\Box_{\zeta}
is the real and complex Laplacian acting on the components. So (
5.5
) is an elliptic PDE system and with some simple modification we can apply Proposition
4.7
. See Lemma
5.7
.

5.2
Some transition lemmas

We first construct the coordinate chart
L
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Endow
\mathbb{R}^{r},\mathbb{C}^{m},\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinate systems
\tau=(\tau^{1},\dots,\tau^{r})
,
w=(w^{1},\dots,w^{m})
,
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
respectively.

Let
\alpha>1
,
\beta>0
. Let
\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
and
(u_{0},v_{0})\in\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}
be as in the assumption of Theorem
5.1
. Then for any
\delta>0
there are neighborhoods
U_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
of
p_{0}
,
V_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}
of
q_{0}
and smooth coordinate charts
(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}):U_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}
,
L^{\prime\prime\prime}:V_{0}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
, such that for the product chart
L=(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime},L^{\prime\prime\prime})
,
L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis
X=[X_{1},\dots,X_{r+m}]^{\top}
with the form (
5.2
) defined on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
, and

\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}<\delta.

(5.6)

Proof.

Let
\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}:\tilde{V}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}\to\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
be an arbitrary smooth coordinate chart near
v_{0}
such that
\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime\prime}(v_{0})=0
. Since
\operatorname{rank}\mathcal{S}=r+m
and
\operatorname{rank}(\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}})=r+2m
, by standard linear algebra argument (see also
[
BCH08
, Page 18]
) we can find a smooth coordinate chart
(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime}):\tilde{U}_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}
such that

\textstyle(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime})(u_{0})=(0,0)\text{ and }\mathcal{S}_{(u_{0},v_{0})}=(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime})^{*}\operatorname{Span}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau^{r}},\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{m}}\big)\big|_{u_{0}}\leq\mathbb{C}T_{u_{0}}\mathfrak{M}.

(5.7)

Therefore, by considering
\tilde{L}:=(\tilde{L}^{\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime},\tilde{L}^{\prime\prime\prime})
and working on
\mathcal{S}_{(u_{0},v_{0})}\leq\mathbb{C}T_{(u_{0},v_{0})}(\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N})
, we have

\textstyle(\tilde{L}_{*}\mathcal{S})_{(0,0,0)}\oplus\operatorname{Span}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}^{1}}\big|_{(0,0,0)},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}^{m}}\big|_{(0,0,0)},\frac{\partial}{\partial s^{1}}\big|_{(0,0,0)},\dots\frac{\partial}{\partial s^{q}}\big|_{(0,0,0)}\big)=\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}.

(5.8)

Applying Lemma
3.9
, since we have (
5.8
), there is a
\lambda_{0}>0
such that
\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\subseteq\tilde{L}(U_{0}\times V_{0})
and we can find a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-local basis
\tilde{X}=[\tilde{X}_{1},\dots,\tilde{X}_{r+m}]^{\top}
of
\tilde{L}_{*}\mathcal{S}
that is defined on
\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
and has the form,

\tilde{X}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{X}^{\prime}\\
\tilde{X}^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&&\tilde{A}^{\prime}&\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime}\\
&I_{m}&\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}&\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\\
\partial_{s}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{on }\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}.

Clearly
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime}=0
and
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}=0
since
\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})\times\mathfrak{N}
. By (
5.7
) we have
\tilde{A}^{\prime}(0)=0
and
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}(0)=0
.

Write
\tilde{A}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}^{\prime}\\
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}
. By scaling (see
[
Str20
, Lemma 5.9 (i)]
or
[
SY21
, Lemma 5.16]
) there is a
C=C(r,m,q,\lambda_{0},\alpha,\beta)>0
such that

\|\tilde{A}(\lambda\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}\leq C\lambda^{\min(\beta,\frac{1}{2})}\|\tilde{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})},\quad\forall\lambda\in(0,\lambda_{0}].

(5.9)

Take
\lambda=(C\|\tilde{A}\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q})})^{-2}\cdot(\frac{\delta}{2})^{2}
. Then the right hand side of (
5.9
) is less than
\delta
.

Take
L:=\lambda\tilde{L}
, we see that
L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}
-local basis
X=[X_{1},\dots,X_{r+m}]^{\top}
with the form

X=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&&A^{\prime}\\
&I_{m}&A^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\tau}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix},\quad\text{where }\begin{pmatrix}A^{\prime}(\tau,w,s)\\
A^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s)\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\tilde{A}^{\prime}(\lambda\tau,\lambda w,\lambda s)\\
\tilde{A}^{\prime\prime}(\lambda\tau,\lambda w,\lambda s)\end{pmatrix}\text{ for }(\tau,w)\in\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},s\in\mathbb{B}^{q}.

So we have
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}=\|\tilde{A}(\lambda\cdot)\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\lambda_{0}\mathbb{B}^{q})}<\delta
as desired.
∎

Next we show that (
5.4
) and (
4.3
) are associated. Here we endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}
with two standard coordinate systems
(\tau,w)=(\tau^{1},\dots,\tau^{r},w^{1},\dots,w^{m})
and
(\sigma,\zeta)=(\sigma^{1},\dots,\sigma^{r},\zeta^{1},\dots,\zeta^{m})
, and we still use
s=(s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
as the standard coordinate system for
\mathbb{R}^{q}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
\varepsilon,\delta>0
,
A:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}
and
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
be as in (the assumptions and consequence) of Proposition
4.3
. Then,

(i)

If
L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis with the form (
5.2
), then
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis with the form (
5.3
).

(ii)

Suppose
\mathcal{S}
is involutive and
H^{\prime\prime}
is a solution to (
4.3
), then the coefficient matrix
B=[B^{\prime},B^{\prime\prime}]^{\top}
in (
5.3
) is a solution to (
5.5
).

Proof.

Following Convention
4.1
, by assumption
H(\tau,w,s)=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
, we have

H^{*}(d\sigma,d\zeta,d\bar{\zeta},ds)=(d\tau,dw,d\bar{w},ds)\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{s}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{s}&I\end{pmatrix}\ \Rightarrow\ \begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\cdot H^{*}\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}.

(5.10)

Therefore taking pushforward of (
5.2
) under
H
we have,

H_{*}X=H_{*}\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&&A^{\prime}\\
&I&A^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\circ H^{\mathrm{Inv}}\right)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}.

The matrix
\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}
is invertible (see Lemma
4.5

(i)
), we see that
H_{*}(L_{*}\mathcal{S})=(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a local basis

H_{*}\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
X^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\right)=\left(\left(I_{r+m}+\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)\circ H^{\mathrm{Inv}}\right)\cdot\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\zeta}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}}\end{pmatrix}.

This gives (
5.3
) and finishes the proof of
(i)
.

Recall the notations
B^{\prime}=(b_{j}^{l})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq r;1\leq l\leq m\end{subarray}}
and
B^{\prime\prime}=(b_{r+k}^{l})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq k,l\leq m\end{subarray}}
. By (
5.3
) we have,

b_{j}^{l}\circ H=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\!\!\begin{pmatrix}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}+A^{\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\\
\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}+A^{\prime\prime}\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{j}^{l},\quad 1\leq j\leq m+r,\quad 1\leq l\leq m.

(5.11)

By (
5.10
) we have, for
1\leq j\leq r
and
1\leq k\leq m
,

\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\sigma^{j}}\right|_{H(\tau,w,s)}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\;\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{j},\ \left.\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}\right|_{H(\tau,w,s)}=\left(\begin{pmatrix}I&H^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\tau}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{w}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{w}\\
&H^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}&\bar{H}^{\prime\prime}_{\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\;\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial w}\\
\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}\end{pmatrix}\right)_{r+m+k}.

(5.12)

Plugging (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) into (
4.3
) we get
\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial b_{j}^{l}}{\partial\sigma^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial b_{r+k}^{l}}{\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}=0
for
1\leq l\leq m
, which is the last row of (
5.5
).

Since
\mathcal{S}
is involutive,
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
is also involutive, by Lemma
3.9

(ii)
,
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
given in (
5.3
) are pairwise commutative. Writing out the condition
[T_{j},T_{j^{\prime}}]=0

(1\leq j<j^{\prime}\leq r)
,
[T_{j},Z_{k}]=0

(1\leq j\leq r,1\leq k\leq m)
and
[Z_{k},Z_{k^{\prime}}]=0

(1\leq k<k^{\prime}\leq m)
in the coordinate components we get the first three rows of (
5.5
), finishing the proof of
(ii)
.
∎

Now we have the PDE system (
5.5
). We show that it satisfies the assumption of Proposition
4.7
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Suppose
B:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}
solves (
5.5
), then
f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s):=B(\sigma,\frac{1}{2}(\xi+i\eta),s)
is defined on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
and
f
solves a PDE with the form
Lf=\Delta_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\Theta(f,\nabla_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}f)
where
L
and
\Theta
satisfy the assumptions of Proposition
4.7
.

Proof.

We can write (
5.5
) as the form
DB=\tilde{\Theta}(B,\nabla_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}B)
where the coefficients of
D
and
\Theta
take value in
\mathbb{C}^{m\times\frac{(r+m)(r+m-1)}{2}+m}
.

By direct computation (see also
[
Str20
, Lemma B.5]
) we have
D^{*}D=\sum_{j=1}^{r}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\sigma^{j}\partial\sigma^{j}}+\sum_{k=1}^{m}\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\zeta^{k}\partial\bar{\zeta}^{k}}=\Delta_{\sigma}+\Box_{\zeta}
, acting on the components of the vector value functions.

Using the real coordinates
\xi=\operatorname{Re}\zeta
,
\eta=\operatorname{Im}\zeta
, we see that
\Box_{\zeta}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{k=1}^{m}(\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\xi^{k}\partial\xi^{k}}+\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial\eta^{k}\partial\eta^{k}})=\frac{1}{4}\Delta_{\xi,\eta}
. So for
f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s)=B(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)
we have
(D^{*}DB)(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)=(\Delta_{\sigma}+\Delta_{\xi,\eta})f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s)
and
\nabla_{\xi,\eta}f(\sigma,\xi,\eta,s)=\frac{1}{2}(\nabla_{\xi,\eta}B)(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)
. Therefore
f
satisfies
\Delta_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}f=D^{*}\tilde{\Theta}(f,(\nabla_{\sigma}f,2\nabla_{\xi,\eta}f))
. Taking
L:=D^{*}
and
\Theta(f,\nabla_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}f):=\tilde{\Theta}(f,(\nabla_{\sigma}f,2\nabla_{\xi,\eta}f))
we finish the proof.
∎

Finally we find the map
G^{\prime\prime}
in (
5.1
) using Proposition
3.31
. We work on
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}
and its “complex extension”
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}\subset\mathbb{C}^{r+2m}
.

We endow
\mathbb{C}^{r+2m}
with standard complex coordinates
(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta})=(\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\sigma}^{r},\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m},\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1},\dots,\boldsymbol{\eta}^{m})
in the way that
\sigma=\operatorname{Re}\boldsymbol{\sigma}
,
\xi=\operatorname{Re}\boldsymbol{\xi}
and
\eta=\operatorname{Re}\boldsymbol{\eta}
. We denote
\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\zeta}}:=\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\xi}}-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\eta}})=[\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\xi}^{1}}-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\eta}^{1}}),\dots,\frac{1}{2}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\xi}^{m}}-i\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\eta}^{m}})]^{\top}
.

Note that in this place, we temporarily forget the original complex structure of
\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\zeta}
. The objects
\boldsymbol{\zeta}=\boldsymbol{\xi}+i\boldsymbol{\eta}
and
\frac{\partial}{\partial\boldsymbol{\zeta}}
are just conventions.

In this setting,
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
given in (
5.3
) are complex vector fields defined on
\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}\subset\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Suppose
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
in (
5.3
) are commutative, and the coefficient matrix function
B:\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\xi,\eta}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m}
has a
(\sigma,\xi,\eta)
-variable holomorphic extension
\mathfrak{B}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m},\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
. Then for any
(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
there is a neighborhood
U_{1}\times V_{1}\subset\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
of
(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})
and a map
G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(U_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
G^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})=0
and

(\operatorname{Span}(T_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,T_{r}(\cdot,s),Z_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,Z_{m}(\cdot,s)))^{\bot}|_{U_{1}}=\operatorname{Span}(dG^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{1},\dots,dG^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{m})\leq\mathbb{C}T^{*}U_{1},\quad\forall\ s\in V_{1}.

Recall the notation of dual bundle in (
3.6
).

Proof.

We define collections of vector fields
\mathfrak{T}=[\mathfrak{T}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{T}_{r}]^{\top}
and
\mathfrak{Z}=[\mathfrak{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{m}]^{\top}
as

\begin{pmatrix}\mathfrak{T}\\
\mathfrak{Z}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}&&\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}\\
&I_{m}&\mathfrak{B}^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\boldsymbol{\sigma}}\\
\partial_{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}\\
\partial_{\bar{\boldsymbol{\zeta}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Here
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}
is the upper
r\times m
block of
\mathfrak{B}
, and
\mathfrak{B}^{\prime\prime}
is the lower
m\times m
block of
\mathfrak{B}
.

Thus
(T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m})
is the “real domain restriction” of
(\mathfrak{T}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{T}_{r},\mathfrak{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{m})
in the sense of Proposition
3.31
. We see that the coefficients of
[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{T}_{j^{\prime}}]
,
[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{Z}_{k}]
and
[\mathfrak{Z}_{k},\mathfrak{Z}_{k^{\prime}}]
are the holomorphic extension those of
[T_{j},T_{j^{\prime}}]
,
[T_{j},Z_{k}]
and
[Z_{k},Z_{k^{\prime}}]
.

By assumption
T_{1},\dots,T_{r},Z_{1},\dots,Z_{m}
are commutative, so
[T_{j},T_{j^{\prime}}]=[T_{j},Z_{k}]=[Z_{k},Z_{k^{\prime}}]=0
. Since a holomorphic function in
\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}
that vanishes in
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}
must be a zero function, we know
[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{T}_{j^{\prime}}]=[\mathfrak{T}_{j},\mathfrak{Z}_{k}]=[\mathfrak{Z}_{k},\mathfrak{Z}_{k^{\prime}}]=0
as well. Thus
\mathfrak{T}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{T}_{r},\mathfrak{Z}_{1},\dots,\mathfrak{Z}_{m}
are commutative.

Now the assumptions of Proposition
3.31
are satisfied. By Proposition
3.31
we can find a neighborhood
\tilde{U}_{1}\times V_{1}\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
of
(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})
and a map
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\tilde{U}_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
such that
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\sigma_{0},\xi_{0},\eta_{0},s_{0})=0
and for
U_{1}:=\tilde{U}_{1}\cap\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}
we have for each
s\in V_{1}
,

(\operatorname{Span}(T_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,T_{r}(\cdot,s),Z_{1}(\cdot,s),\dots,Z_{m}(\cdot,s)))^{\bot}|_{U_{1}}=\operatorname{Span}(d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{1}|_{U_{1}}),\dots,d(\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s)^{m}|_{U_{1}}))\leq\mathbb{C}T^{*}U_{1}.

Clearly
\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}|_{U_{1}\times V_{1}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(U_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
holds. Thus,
G^{\prime\prime}:=\widetilde{G}^{\prime\prime}|_{U_{1}\times V_{1}}
is as desired.
∎

5.3
Proof of Theorem
5.1

In the proof we often consider the local bases of cotangent subbundles.

Convention 5.9
.

Let
M
and
N
be two smooth manifolds, let
\mathcal{T}\leq(\mathbb{C}TM)\times N
be a continuous subbundle. Let
\mu=(\mu^{1},\dots,\mu^{n}):M\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\nu=(\nu^{1},\dots,\nu^{q}):N\to\mathbb{R}^{q}
be two smooth coordinate charts and let
f=(f^{1},\dots,f^{n-r}):M\times N\to\mathbb{C}^{n-r}
be a continuous map which is
C^{1}
in
M
. We say
\mathcal{T}^{\bot}
is spanned by
df^{1},\dots,df^{n-r},d\nu^{1},\dots,d\nu^{q}
, denoted by
\mathcal{T}^{\bot}=\operatorname{Span}(df,d\nu)
, if

\operatorname{Span}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\partial f^{i}}{\partial\mu^{j}}(x,s)d\mu^{j}|_{x}\Big)_{i=1}^{r}=\{\theta\in T^{*}_{x}M:\langle\theta,X\rangle=0,\ \forall X\in\mathcal{T}_{(x,s)}\},\quad\forall\ u\in M,\ s\in N.

(5.13)

Remark 5.10
.

When
\beta>1
and when
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
, (
5.13
) coincides with the classical notion
\mathcal{T}^{\bot}=\operatorname{Span}(df,d\nu)
since
\operatorname{Span}(df,d\nu)=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mu}d\mu+\frac{\partial f}{\partial\nu}d\nu,d\nu)=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mu}d\mu,d\nu)
.

The convention is used specifically for the case
\beta\leq 1
, since when
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}
, the collection of differentials
df
is no longer pointwise defined, while
\frac{\partial f}{\partial\mu}\cdot d\mu\equiv df\pmod{d\nu}
and the left hand side is still pointwise.

We now begin the proof.
On the space
\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\zeta}
, we write
\xi=\operatorname{Re}\zeta
and
\eta=\operatorname{Im}\zeta
.

By Lemma
5.7
and Proposition
4.7
, along with a scaling, we can find a
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(r,m,q,\alpha)>0
such that

(B.1)

If
B\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\eta,\xi},\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
satisfies (
5.5
) with

\|B\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}L^{\infty}\cap\mathscr{C}^{-1}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q})}<\varepsilon_{0},

(5.14)

then
B(\sigma,\frac{\xi+i\eta}{2},s)
has a
(\sigma,\xi,\eta)
-holomorphic extension
\mathfrak{B}(\boldsymbol{\sigma},\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\xi},\frac{1}{2}\boldsymbol{\eta},s)\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\mathbb{H}^{r+2m}_{\boldsymbol{\sigma},\boldsymbol{\xi},\boldsymbol{\eta}},\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
.

For such
\varepsilon_{0}>0
, we can find a
\delta_{0}>0
such that the results of Proposition
4.3
are satisfied with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
,
\delta=\delta_{0}
.

For such
\delta_{0}>0
, by Lemma
5.5
there is a smooth coordinate chart
L:=((L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}),L^{\prime\prime\prime}):U_{0}\times V_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
near
(u_{0},v_{0})
such that,

(L.1)

(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime}):U_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}
and
L^{\prime\prime\prime}:V_{0}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
are both smooth coordinate charts, and
L(u_{0},v_{0})=(0,0)
.

(L.2)

L_{*}\mathcal{S}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
-local basis with form (
5.2
), where (
5.6
) is satisfied with
\delta=\delta_{0}
.

Now
\|A\|_{\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})}<\delta_{0}
from (
5.6
). By Proposition
4.3
and Corollary
4.4
we can find a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}
-map
H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\sigma}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
that has the form
H(\tau,w,s)=(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
such that

(H.1)

H:\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\tau,w}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}\to\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
is homeomorphism, and
H,H^{\mathrm{Inv}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

(H.2)

H(\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{B}^{q})\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
.

(H.3)

H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}(\mathbb{B}^{r+2m},\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m})
.

(H.4)

H^{\prime\prime}
solves (
4.3
).

(H.5)

(
4.4
) is satisfied with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
.

Here
(H.1)
shows that
(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}
is defined on
\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}_{\sigma,\zeta}\times\mathbb{B}^{q}_{s}
.

By Lemma
5.6

(ii)
,
(H.4)
implies that
B
solves (
5.5
). By comparing (
4.2
) and (
5.3
) we see that
B=\Lambda[A,H^{\prime\prime}]\circ H^{\mathrm{Inv}}
, so
(H.5)
implies (
5.14
) with
\varepsilon=\varepsilon_{0}
. Thus we get the result
(B.1)
: a matrix-valued function
\mathfrak{B}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{H}^{r+2m},\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q};\mathbb{C}^{(r+m)\times m})
.

Applying Lemma
5.8
on
H\circ L(u_{0},v_{0})=H(0,0)\in\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
, we can find a neighborhood
U_{1}\times V_{1}\subseteq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{r+2m}\times\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{B}^{q}
of
H(0,0)
and a
G^{\prime\prime}:U_{1}\times V_{1}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}
such that

(G.1)

G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}\mathscr{C}^{\beta}(U_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
G^{\prime\prime}(H(0,0))=0
. In particular
G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{(\sigma,\zeta),s}
and
\nabla_{\sigma,\zeta}G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{(\sigma,\zeta),s}
.

(G.2)

For each
s\in V_{1}
,
\operatorname{Span}d(G^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,s))=\operatorname{Span}(T(\cdot,s),Z(\cdot,s))|_{U_{1}}^{\bot}=(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}|_{U_{1}\times\{s\}}^{\bot}
. Equivalently we have
\operatorname{Span}(dG^{\prime\prime},ds)=(H\circ L)_{*}\mathcal{S}|_{U_{1}\times V_{1}}^{\bot}
in the sense of Convention
5.9
.

Therefore,
(\operatorname{Span}d(G^{\prime\prime}\circ H),ds)|_{H^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})}=L_{*}\mathcal{S}|_{H^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})}
in the sense of Convention
5.9
, which means

\operatorname{Span}(d(G^{\prime\prime}\circ H\circ L),dL^{\prime\prime\prime})|_{(H\circ L)^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})}=\mathcal{S}|_{(H\circ L)^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})}^{\bot}\text{ in the sense of Convention \ref{Conv::Key::CoSpan}}.

(5.15)

Next, we claim that

(L.3)

\big(dL^{\prime},d(\operatorname{Re}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H\circ L(\cdot,v_{0})),d(\operatorname{Im}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H\circ L(\cdot,v_{0}))\big)
is a collection of
(r+2m)
-real differentials that are linearly independent at
u_{0}\in\mathfrak{M}
.

Indeed, we have
\operatorname{Span}(d(\overline{G^{\prime\prime}}\circ H),ds)=L_{*}\bar{\mathcal{S}}^{\bot}
in the sense of Convention
5.9
, which means for each
s\in V_{1}
,

\operatorname{Span}(d(\operatorname{Re}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H(\cdot,s)),d(\operatorname{Im}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H(\cdot,s))^{\bot}=\operatorname{Span}(d(G^{\prime\prime}\circ H(\cdot,s)),d(\overline{G^{\prime\prime}}\circ H(\cdot,s)))^{\bot}=L_{*}(\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}})|_{H^{-1}(U_{1}\times\{s\})},

has rank
r=\dim\mathfrak{M}-(r+2m)
. Therefore,
(d(\operatorname{Re}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H(\cdot,s),d(\operatorname{Im}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H(\cdot,s))
is a linearly independent collection in the domain for all
s\in V_{1}
.

On the other hand, by (
5.2
) we have

L_{*}\mathcal{S}=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}+A^{\prime}\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\frac{\partial}{\partial w}+A^{\prime\prime}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}})
, so
L_{*}(\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}})=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau}+\frac{1}{2}A^{\prime}\frac{\partial}{\partial w}+\frac{1}{2}\bar{A}^{\prime}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}
, which means
L_{*}(\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}})^{\bot}\cap\operatorname{Span}d\tau=\{0\}
. We conclude that
(d\tau,d(\operatorname{Re}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H(\cdot,s)),d(\operatorname{Im}G^{\prime\prime}\circ H(\cdot,s))
is a linearly independent collection in the domain for all
s\in V_{1}
. Since
dL^{\prime}=L^{*}d\tau
and
dL^{\prime\prime\prime}=L^{*}ds
, taking compositions with
L
we obtain
(L.3)
.

Now endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinates
(t,z,s)=(t^{1},\dots,t^{r},z^{1},\dots,z^{m},s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
. We take

F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):=(L^{\prime},\overline{G^{\prime\prime}}\circ H\circ L,L^{\prime\prime\prime}):(H\circ L)^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}.

(5.16)

By
(L.1)
,
(H.3)
,
(G.1)
and Lemma
2.32

(i)
we have

(F.1)

F^{\prime}(u_{0})=0
,
F^{\prime\prime}(u_{0},v_{0})=0
,
F^{\prime\prime\prime}(v_{0})=0
. And for every product neighborhood
U\times V\subseteq(H\circ L)^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})
of
(u_{0},v_{0})
, we have
F^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{R}^{r})
,
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U,V;\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(V;\mathbb{R}^{q})
.

In particular
(1)
is satisfied, since
L^{\prime\prime\prime}
is already a smooth coordinate chart.

By
(F.1)

(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime})\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}(U,V;\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m})
for every
U\times V\subseteq(H\circ L)^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})
. By
(L.3)
,
(dF^{\prime},dF^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,v_{0}))
has full rank
r+2m
in the domain.
Therefore, by Lemma
2.32

(iii)
, we can find a small enough neighborhood
U\times V\subseteq(H\circ L)^{-1}(U_{1}\times V_{1})
of
(u_{0},v_{0})
, such that
F:U\times V\to F(U\times V)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
is homeomorphism, and

(F.2)

F^{\mathrm{Inv}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(t,z),s}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathfrak{M}\times\mathfrak{N})
whenever
\Omega^{\prime}_{t}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{z}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{s}\subseteq F(U\times V)
.

Define
\Phi:=F^{\mathrm{Inv}}:\Omega\to U\times V
where
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime}_{s}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{z}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{s}
is an arbitrary product neighborhood of
(0,0,0)
in the assumption of Theorem
5.1
. We are going to show that
F
and
\Phi
are as desired.

Firstly, by
(F.1)
, we get
\Phi^{\prime\prime}=(L^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{Inv}}=(F^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\mathrm{Inv}}
is a smooth parameterization, which is the result
(5)
.

By
(F.1)
and
(F.2)
we know
F\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
,
\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
. Thus
(9)
holds and
F
,
\Phi
are both homeomorphic to their images respectively. Since
F(\cdot,v)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
,
\Phi(\cdot,s)\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
for each
v
and
s
, we know
(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,v))
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-chart and
\Phi^{\prime}(\cdot,s)
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1}
-parameterization, which gives
(2)
and
(6)
.

To prove
(3)
and
(7)
, we use
\widehat{U}:=(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime})(U)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\tau}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}
,
\widehat{V}:=L^{\prime\prime\prime}(V)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
,
\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}:=F^{\prime\prime}\circ L^{-1}
and
\widehat{\Phi}:=L\circ\Phi
.
Since
(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime})
and
L^{\prime\prime\prime}
are smooth charts, it is equivalent but more convenient to work on the chart
\widehat{F}:\widehat{U}\times\widehat{V}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
and the parameterization
\widehat{\Phi}:\Omega\to\widehat{U}\times\widehat{V}
:
it suffices to show the following

\begin{gathered}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}(\widehat{U},\widehat{V};\mathbb{C}^{m}),\quad\nabla_{\tau,w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}(\widehat{U},\widehat{V};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times m}),\\
\widehat{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(t,z),s}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\widehat{U}\times\widehat{V}),\quad\nabla_{t,z}\widehat{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times(r+2m)}).\end{gathered}

(5.17)

Here for every vector field
X\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\mathfrak{M};T\mathfrak{M})
,
(L^{\prime},L^{\prime\prime})_{*}X
must be the smooth linear combinations of
\frac{\partial}{\partial\tau},\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}
on
\widehat{U}
. Thus
\nabla_{\tau,w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
implies
XF^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}
.

Proof of (
5.17
)
: By (
5.16
) we have
\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s)=\overline{G^{\prime\prime}}(H(\tau,w,s))=\overline{G^{\prime\prime}}(\tau,H^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
.
By
(H.3)
and
(G.1)
we have
G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{(\sigma,\zeta),s}
,
H\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
,
\nabla_{\sigma,\zeta}G^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\beta}_{(\sigma,\zeta),s}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}H\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
. Therefore, by Lemma
2.32

(i)
we get
\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
and
\nabla_{\tau,w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}=((\nabla_{\sigma,\zeta}\overline{G^{\prime\prime}})\circ H)\cdot\nabla_{\tau,w}H\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}\cdot\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
.

Since
F\circ L^{-1}(\tau,w,s)=(\tau,\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s),s)
, we see that
\widehat{\Phi}
has the form

\widehat{\Phi}(t,z,s)=(t,\widehat{\Psi}(t,z,s),s),\quad\text{where }\widehat{\Psi}:\Omega^{\prime}_{t}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{z}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{s}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w},\quad\widehat{\Psi}(t,z,s):=\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}(t,\cdot,s)^{\mathrm{Inv}}(z).

Since
\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(\tau,w),s}
, applying Lemma
2.32

(iii)
to the map
[(\tau,w),s)\mapsto(\tau,\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}(\tau,w,s))]
we get
\widehat{\Psi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(t,z),s}
.

Using the chain rules on
z=\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}(\widehat{\Phi}(t,z,s))=\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}(t,\widehat{\Psi}(t,z,s),s)
we have (recall
\nabla_{w}=[\frac{\partial}{\partial w},\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}}]^{\top}
),

\displaystyle I_{2m}

\displaystyle=((\nabla_{w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime})\circ\widehat{\Phi})\cdot\nabla_{z}\widehat{\Psi},

\displaystyle 0_{r\times m}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\tau}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\circ\widehat{\Phi}+((\nabla_{w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime})\circ\widehat{\Phi})\cdot\nabla_{t}\widehat{\Psi};

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\quad\nabla_{z}\widehat{\Psi}

\displaystyle=(\nabla_{w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\circ\widehat{\Phi},

\displaystyle\nabla_{t}\widehat{\Psi}

\displaystyle=((\nabla_{w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\cdot\nabla_{\tau}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime})\circ\widehat{\Phi}.

Since
\nabla_{\tau,w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
, by Lemma
2.32

(ii)
we have
(\nabla_{w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
thus
(\nabla_{w}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime})^{-1}\cdot\nabla_{\tau}\widehat{F}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(\tau,w),s}
. Applying Lemma
2.32

(i)
with
\widehat{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{(t,z),s}
, we get
\nabla_{t,z}\widehat{\Psi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(t,z),s}
. Therefore
\nabla_{t,z}\widehat{\Phi}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(t,z),s}
finishing the proof of (
5.17
) and hence the proof of
(3)
and
(7)
.

Now by
(F.1)
and
(7)
we see that
F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\beta}_{u,v}
and
\nabla_{t,z}\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{(t,z),s}
. By Lemma
2.32

(i)
we see that
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}\circ F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U,V;T\mathfrak{M})
and
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{k}}\circ F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U,V;\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})
for
1\leq j\leq r
and
1\leq k\leq m
. Thus
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{j}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}\circ F
,
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{k}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{k}}\circ F
are both well-defined vector fields and have regularity
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}\cap\mathscr{C}^{\beta-}=\mathscr{C}^{\min(\alpha,\beta-)}
.

By definition,
dF^{\prime}=F^{*}dt
,
dF^{\prime\prime\prime}=F^{*}ds
,
dF^{\prime\prime}=F^{*}dz
and
d\overline{F^{\prime\prime}}=F^{*}d\bar{z}
. So by (
5.15
) and (
5.16
), in the sense of Convention
5.9
,

\textstyle\operatorname{Span}(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})=\operatorname{Span}(F^{*}d\bar{z},F^{*}ds)^{\bot}=\operatorname{Span}(d\overline{F^{\prime\prime}},dF^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\bot}=\mathcal{S}|_{U\times V}.

This proves
(4)
. Immediately
(8)
follows.
Now the whole proof is complete.∎

6
The Rough Complex Frobenius Theorem: Statements and Proofs

Now we give more general statements for Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
with proofs.

Recall Definitions
2.10
,
2.15
and
3.2
for the
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}
-spaces, Definition
3.5
for a local basis, and (
1.2
) for the column and matrix convention of Jacobian matrices.

We endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinate system
(t,z,s)=(t^{1},\dots,t^{r},z^{1},\dots,z^{m},s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
.

The refinement of Theorem
1.1
is the following.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\kappa\in(1,\infty]
satisfy
\alpha\leq\beta\leq\kappa-1
, and let
r,m,q
be nonnegative integers. Let
\mathfrak{M}
be a
(r+2m+q)
-dimensional
\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
-manifold.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-complex Frobenius structure over
\mathfrak{M}
that has complex rank
r+m
, such that
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-subbundle and has complex rank
r+2m
.

Then for any
p_{0}\in\mathfrak{M}
there is an open neighborhood
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
of
(0,0,0)
and a map
\Phi:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}
such that:

(1)

\Phi:\Omega\to\Phi(\Omega)
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-diffeomorphism and
\Phi(0)=p_{0}
.

(2)

\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathfrak{M})
.

(3)

\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{1}},\cdots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{r}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\alpha-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};T\mathfrak{M})
, and
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{m}},\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial\bar{z}^{m}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\alpha-}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M})
.

(4)

For any
u\in\Omega
, the complex subspace
\mathcal{S}_{\Phi(u)}
is spanned by
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{1}}(u),\cdots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{r}}(u),\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{1}}(u),\dots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{m}}(u)\in\mathbb{C}T_{\Phi(u)}\mathfrak{M}
.

For the coordinate side, set
U:=\Phi(\Omega)\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
and let
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):U\to\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
be the inverse map of
\Phi
. Then:

(5)

F
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-coordinate chart of
\mathfrak{M}
near
p_{0}
and
F(p_{0})=(0,0,0)
.

(6)

F^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{R}^{r})
,
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{C}^{m})
and
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{R}^{q})
.

(7)

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{m}}
are
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
-vector fields defined on
U
.

(8)

\mathcal{S}|_{U}
has a local basis
(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{m}})
.

In particular,

(9)

\mathcal{S}^{\bot}|_{U}
has a local basis
(F^{*}d\bar{z}^{1},\dots,F^{*}d\bar{z}^{m},F^{*}ds^{1},\dots,F^{*}ds^{q})
.

(10)

(\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}})|_{U}
has a local basis
(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}})
.

(11)

(\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}})|_{U}
has a local basis
(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{m}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}^{m}})
.

The refinement of Theorem
1.2
is the following

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\kappa>1
,
r,m,q\geq 0
,
\mathfrak{M}
and
\mathcal{S}\leq\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M}
be the same as in Theorem
6.1
.

Suppose in addition to the assumptions of Theorem
6.1
, there is a
\gamma\in(1,\infty]
such that
\alpha+1\leq\gamma\leq\kappa-1
and
\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
-subbundle.

Then for any
p_{0}\in\mathfrak{M}
there are an open neighborhood
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
of
(0,0,0)
, and a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-map
\Phi:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}
, such that in additional to all the consequences in Theorem
6.1
,

(12)

\Phi\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+1,\alpha+1,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathfrak{M})
.

(13)

\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{1}},\cdots,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{r}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma,\alpha+1,\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega^{\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};T\mathfrak{M})
.

(14)

For
F=\Phi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
, the vector fields
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}}
defined on
U=\Phi(\Omega)
are all
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
.

Some of the results are directly implied by others, we mark them as below.

Remark 6.3
.

(i)

The case
\alpha=\infty
(which implies
\beta=\gamma=\kappa=\infty
) is the classical result
[
Nir57
]
(see Proposition
1.0
).

(ii)

(7)
is implied by
(3)
and
(5)
.

We have
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{j}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}\circ F
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{k}}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{k}}\circ F
for
1\leq j\leq r
and
1\leq k\leq m
.
(3)
shows that
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}},\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{k}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
and
(5)
shows that
F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
. Taking compositions we get
\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}\circ F,\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{k}}\circ F\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
. ∎

(iii)

(4)
and
(8)
are equivalent.

This is because
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{j}}|_{p}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial t^{j}}(F(p))
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{k}}|_{p}=\frac{\partial\Phi}{\partial z^{k}}(F(p))
for
p\in U
,
1\leq j\leq r
and
1\leq k\leq m
. ∎

(iv)

(9)
,
(10)
and
(11)
are all implied by
(8)
.

Since
(F^{*}dt,F^{*}dz,F^{*}d\bar{z},F^{*}ds)
is the dual basis of
(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s})
, and by
(8)

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
span
\mathcal{S}|_{U}
. Taking the dual bundle, we get that
F^{*}d\bar{z},F^{*}ds
span
\mathcal{S}^{\bot}|_{U}
.

For the complex conjugate of
(8)
,
\bar{\mathcal{S}}|_{U}
has local basis
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}
. Taking intersection we get that
\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}}|_{U}
is spanned by
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
. Taking union we get that
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}|_{U}
is spanned by
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}}
. ∎

The proofs of Theorems
6.1
and
6.2
are combinations of the real Frobenius theorem, the Theorem
3.27
or Corollary
3.29
, and the estimate of the parameterized elliptic structures, the Theorem
5.1
.

Proof of Theorem
6.1
.

By Remark
6.3

(i)
only the case
\alpha<\infty
needs a proof.

We fix the point
p_{0}\in\mathfrak{M}
.
First we apply real Frobenius theorem, the Corollary
3.23

\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}
. We endow
\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinate system
(\nu,\lambda)=(\nu^{1},\dots,\nu^{r+2m},\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{q})
.

Since
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
and has rank
r+2m
, we can find a neighborhood
\Omega_{1}=\Omega^{\prime}_{1}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}_{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{\lambda}
of
(0,0)
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-parameterization
\Phi_{1}:\Omega_{1}\to\mathfrak{M}
, such that by taking
U_{1}:=\Phi_{1}(\Omega_{1})
and
F_{1}=(F_{1}^{\prime},F_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}):U_{1}\to\mathbb{R}^{r+2m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
,
F_{1}:=\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{Inv}}
, we have

(S1.1)

\Phi_{1}(0,0)=p_{0}
.

(S1.2)

\Phi_{1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+1,\beta}_{\nu,\lambda,\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{1}^{\prime},\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathfrak{M})
and
\nabla_{\nu}\Phi_{1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{1};T\mathfrak{M}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{r+2m})
.

(S1.3)

F_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U_{1};\mathbb{R}^{r+2m})
and
F_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U_{1};\mathbb{R}^{q})
.

(S1.4)

(\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}})|_{U_{1}}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
-local basis
(F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{r+2m}})
.

Now
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}=(F_{1})_{*}\mathcal{S}\leq\mathbb{C}T\Omega_{1}
. By
(S1.4)
,
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}\leq\Phi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}})=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{r+2m}})|_{\Omega_{1}}=(\mathbb{C}T\Omega_{1}^{\prime})\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Since
F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{r+2m}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}\subseteq\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
, by Lemma
3.17

\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-subbundle of
(\mathbb{C}T\Omega_{1}^{\prime})\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.

Now Theorem
5.1
applies to
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}\leq(\mathbb{C}T\Omega_{1}^{\prime})\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
.
We endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard real and complex coordinate system
(t,z,s)=(t^{1},\dots,t^{r},z^{1},\dots,z^{m},s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
. By Theorem
5.1
, we can find a neighborhood
\Omega=\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
of
(0,0,0)
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-parameterization
\Phi_{2}=(\Phi_{2}^{\prime},\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}):\Omega\to\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
, such that by taking
U_{2}:=\Phi_{2}(\Omega)
and
F_{2}=(F_{2}^{\prime},F_{2}^{\prime\prime},F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}):=\Phi_{2}^{\mathrm{Inv}}:U_{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
,

(S1.5)

\Phi_{2}(0,0,0)=(0,0)
.

(S1.6)

\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s):\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}\to\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}
is a smooth parameterization that does not depend on
(t,z)
.

(S1.7)

\Phi_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\alpha}_{(t,z),s}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\Omega_{1}^{\prime})
and
\nabla_{t,z}\Phi_{2}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\alpha-}(\Omega^{\prime}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime},\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}^{(r+2m)\times(r+2m)})
.

(S1.8)

F_{2}^{\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime}(\nu)\in C^{\infty}
does not depend on
\lambda
; and
F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)\in C^{\infty}
does not depend on
\nu
.

(S1.9)

\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}|_{U_{2}}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
-local basis
(F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}},F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{m}})
.

We now define
\Phi:=\Phi_{1}\circ\Phi_{2}:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}
,
U:=\Phi(\Omega)
and
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):=\Phi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
.

Immediately by
(S1.1)
and
(S1.5)
we have
(1)
and
(5)
.

By
(S1.4)
and
(S1.9)
we have
\mathcal{S}=\Phi_{1}^{*}\operatorname{Span}(F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})=\operatorname{Span}(F_{1}^{*}F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F_{1}^{*}F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})=\operatorname{Span}(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})
, which is
(8)
. By Remark
6.3

(iii)
we get
(4)
.

By
(S1.6)
we have
\Phi(t,z,s)=\Phi_{1}(\Phi_{2}^{\prime}(t,z,s),\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s))
. By chain rule we have

\nabla_{t,z}\Phi(t,z,s)=((\nabla_{\nu}\Phi_{1})\circ\Phi_{2})(t,z,s))\cdot\nabla_{t,z}\Phi_{2}^{\prime}(t,z,s).

Thus
(2)
and
(3)
follow by applying Lemma
2.32

(i)
with
(S1.2)
and
(S1.7)
.

Clearly
F^{\prime\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime\prime}\circ F_{1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
. By
(S1.3)
and
(S1.8)
we have
F^{\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime}\circ F_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
and
F^{\prime\prime\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}\circ F_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
, giving the result
(6)
.

The rest of the results in Theorem
6.1
follow from Remark
6.3
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
6.2
.

We use a construction that is slightly different to the proof of Theorem
6.1
. Instead of applying Corollary
3.29
, we apply Theorem
3.27
in the first step.

We endow
\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{R}^{2m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinate system
(\mu,\nu,\lambda)=(\mu^{1},\dots,\mu^{r},\nu^{1},\dots,\nu^{2m},\lambda^{1},\dots,\lambda^{q})
.

Since
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
has rank
r+2m
and
\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
has rank
r
, we can find a neighborhood
\Omega_{1}=\Omega^{\prime}_{1}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime}_{1}\times\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\mu}\times\mathbb{R}^{2m}_{\nu}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{\lambda}
of
(0,0,0)
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\min(\beta,\gamma)}
-parameterization
\Phi_{1}:\Omega_{1}\to\mathfrak{M}
, such that by taking
U_{1}:=\Phi_{1}(\Omega_{1})
and
F_{1}=(F_{1}^{\prime},F_{1}^{\prime\prime},F_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}):U_{1}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}\times\mathbb{R}^{2m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
as
F_{1}:=\Phi_{1}^{\mathrm{Inv}}
, we have

(S2.1)

\Phi_{1}(0,0,0)=p_{0}
,

(S2.2)

\Phi_{1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+1,\min(\gamma,\beta+1),\min(\gamma,\beta)}_{\mu,\nu,\lambda,\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{1}^{\prime},\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime},\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathfrak{M})
and
\nabla_{\mu}\Phi_{1}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma,\min(\gamma,\beta+1),\min(\gamma,\beta)}_{\mathrm{loc}}(\Omega_{1}^{\prime},\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime},\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime};T\mathfrak{M}\otimes\mathbb{R}^{r})
.

(S2.3)

F_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U_{1};\mathbb{R}^{r})
,
F_{1}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U_{1};\mathbb{R}^{2m})
and
F_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U_{1};\mathbb{R}^{q})
.

(S2.4)

On
U_{1}
,
F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{r}}
are
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
-vector fields and
F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{2m}}
are
\mathscr{C}^{\min(\gamma-1,\beta)}
-vector fields.

(S2.5)

(\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}})|_{U_{1}}=\operatorname{Span}(F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{r}})
, and
(\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}})|_{U_{1}}=\operatorname{Span}(F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{r}},F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{2m}})
.

By
(S2.4)
and
(S2.5)
,
\mathcal{S}
is contained in the span of
F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{r}},F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{1}},\dots,F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\nu^{2m}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\min(\beta,\gamma-1)}
. By assumption
\min(\gamma-1,\beta)\geq\alpha
, using Lemma
3.17
we see that
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}\leq\mathbb{C}T\Omega_{1}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-subbundle

By the standard linear algebra argument we can find a linear complex coordinate system
w=(w^{1},\dots,w^{m})
on
\mathbb{R}^{2m}_{\nu}
such that

\textstyle(\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S})_{(0,0)}=\operatorname{Span}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{1}}|_{(0,0)},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{r}}|_{(0,0)},\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{1}}|_{(0,0)},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial w^{m}}|_{(0,0)}\big)\leq\mathbb{C}T_{(0,0)}(\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w}).

In particular
(\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S})_{(0,0)}\oplus\operatorname{Span}\big(\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}^{1}}|_{(0,0)},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}^{m}}|_{(0,0)},\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda^{1}}|_{(0,0)},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\lambda^{q}}|_{(0,0)})=\mathbb{C}T_{(0,0)}(\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\mu}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{w})
.
Applying Lemma
3.9
, we can find a neighborhood
W=W^{\prime}\times W^{\prime\prime}\times W^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
of
(0,0,0)\in\Omega
such that
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}
has the following local basis on
W
,

X=\begin{pmatrix}X^{\prime}\\
X^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}I&&A^{\prime}&B^{\prime}\\
&I&A^{\prime\prime}&B^{\prime\prime}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{\mu}\\
\partial_{w}\\
\partial_{\bar{w}}\\
\partial_{\lambda}\end{pmatrix}.

where
A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime},B^{\prime},B^{\prime\prime}
are
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-matrix functions defined in
W
.

Since
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}\leq\mathbb{C}T(\Omega_{1}^{\prime}\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime})\times\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
, we have
B^{\prime}\equiv 0
and
B^{\prime\prime}\equiv 0
. Since
\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{1}},\dots,\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{r}}
are sections of
\Phi_{1}^{*}(\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}})\subset\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}
, by Lemma
3.9

(i)
we have
A^{\prime}=0
, i.e.
X_{j}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{j}}
for
1\leq j\leq r
.

By Lemma
3.9

(ii)
, since
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}
is involutive, we see that
[X_{j},X_{k}]=0
for
1\leq j\leq r<k\leq r+m
. So
\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{j}}A^{\prime\prime}(\mu,w,s)=0
for
1\leq j\leq r
in the domain, which means
A^{\prime\prime}(\mu,w,s)=A^{\prime\prime}(w,s)
.

Define a complex tangent subbundle
\mathcal{T}\leq\mathbb{C}T(W^{\prime\prime}\times W^{\prime\prime\prime})
as
\mathcal{T}:=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial}{\partial w}+A^{\prime\prime}(w,s)\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{w}})
. We see that
\mathcal{T}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-involutive subbundle of rank
m
and satisfies

\mathcal{T}\cap\bar{\mathcal{T}}=0,\quad\mathcal{T}+\bar{\mathcal{T}}=(\mathbb{C}TW^{\prime\prime})\times W^{\prime\prime\prime},\quad(\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S})|_{W}=(\mathbb{C}TW^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{T}.

(6.1)

Endow
\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
with standard coordinate system
(z,s)=(z^{1},\dots,z^{m},s^{1},\dots,s^{q})
. By Theorem
5.1
(by taking
r=0
in this theorem), we can find a neighborhood
\Omega_{2}=\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}\times\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}
of
(0,0)
and a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-parameterization
\Phi_{2}=(\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime},\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}):\Omega_{2}\to W^{\prime\prime}\times W^{\prime\prime\prime}
, such that by taking
U_{2}:=\Phi_{2}(\Omega_{2})
and
F_{2}=(F_{2}^{\prime\prime},F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}):U_{2}\to\mathbb{C}^{m}\times\mathbb{R}^{q}
,
F_{2}:=\Phi_{2}^{\mathrm{Inv}}
, we have

(S2.6)

\Phi_{2}(0,0,0)=(0,0)
.

(S2.7)

\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s):\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{q}_{s}\to\Omega_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}
is a smooth parameterization that does not depend on
z
.

(S2.8)

\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+1,\alpha}_{z,s}(\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime},\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime};W^{\prime\prime})
and
\nabla_{z}\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\alpha-}(\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime},\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}^{2m\times 2m})

(S2.9)

F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(\lambda)\in C^{\infty}
does not depend on
(\mu,\nu)
; and
F_{2}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(U_{2};\mathbb{C}^{m})
.

(S2.10)

\mathcal{T}|_{U_{2}}
has a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
-local basis
(F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{m}})
.

Define
\Omega^{\prime}:=W^{\prime}
,
\Omega^{\prime\prime}:=\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime}
,
\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}:=\Omega_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
\Phi:\Omega\to\mathfrak{M}
as

\Phi(t,z,s):=\Phi_{1}(t,\Phi_{2}(t,z))=\Phi_{1}(t,\Phi^{\prime\prime}_{2}(z,s),\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)),\quad t\in\Omega^{\prime},z\in\Omega^{\prime\prime},s\in\Omega^{\prime\prime\prime}.

(6.2)

We define
U:=\Phi(\Omega)
and
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):=\Phi^{\mathrm{Inv}}
. Immediately by
(S2.1)
and
(S2.6)
we have
(1)
and
(5)
.

Taking the chain rules on (
6.2
) we have

\nabla_{t}\Phi(t,z,s)=\nabla_{\mu}\Phi_{1}(t,\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}(z,s),\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s)),\quad\nabla_{z}\Phi(t,z,s)=(\nabla_{\nu}\Phi_{1})(t,\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}(z,s),\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s))\cdot\nabla_{z}\Phi_{2}(z,s).

(6.3)

Based on
(S2.2)
and
(S2.8)
, applying Lemma
2.32

(i)
to (
6.2
) and (
6.3
), we get
(12)
,
(3)
and
(13)
. Note that
(12)
is stronger than
(2)
.

By (
6.2
),
(S2.3)
and
(S2.9)
we see that
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime})
satisfies

F^{\prime}=F_{1}^{\prime}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{r}}\circ F_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{R}^{r}),\quad F^{\prime\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime\prime}\circ F_{1}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{C}^{m}),\quad F^{\prime\prime\prime}=F_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}\circ F_{1}^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{R}^{q}).

(6.4)

This gives
(6)
.

By (
6.3
), we get
\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{j}}\Phi(t,z,s)=\frac{\partial\Phi_{1}}{\partial\mu^{j}}(t,\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime}(z,s),\Phi_{2}^{\prime\prime\prime}(s))=(\Phi_{1})_{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{j}}|_{\Phi(t,z,s)}
for
1\leq j\leq r
. So
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{j}}=F_{1}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu^{j}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
for
1\leq j\leq r
, finishing the proof of
(14)
.

By (
6.1
) and
(S2.10)
we see that
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial}{\partial\mu},F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})
, so by writing
\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{r}}:\mathbb{R}^{r}_{\mu}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}
as the identity we have
\Phi_{1}^{*}\mathcal{S}=\operatorname{Span}(\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{r}}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})
. Using (
6.4
) we have
F=(\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{r}},F_{2})\circ F_{1}
, taking pushforward of
\Phi_{1}
we get
\mathcal{S}|_{U}=F_{1}^{*}\operatorname{Span}(\operatorname{id}_{\mathbb{R}^{r}}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F_{2}^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})|_{U}=\operatorname{Span}(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z})
, finishing the proof of
(8)
.

The rest of the results follow from Remark
6.3
as well.
∎

7
The Sharpness of Coordinates and Coordinate Vector Fields

Fixed four numbers
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\kappa>1
such that
\alpha\leq\min(\beta,\gamma)
and
\kappa\geq\max(\beta,\gamma)+1
. Consider all possible complex Frobenius structures
\mathcal{S}\leq\mathbb{C}T\mathfrak{M}
among all
\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
-manifolds
\mathfrak{M}
such that
\mathcal{S}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
,
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
and
\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
.

Fix a point
p\in\mathfrak{M}
, let
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):U\subseteq\mathfrak{M}\to\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}_{s}
be any desired
C^{1}
-coordinate chart near a fixed point
p
that represents
\mathcal{S}
, that is,
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t^{r}},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{1}},\dots,F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{m}}
span
\mathcal{S}
in
U
.

Recall the notation
\mathscr{C}^{\mu+}=\bigcup_{\nu>\mu}\mathscr{C}^{\nu}
for
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
.

We list the best Hölder-Zygmund regularities the six objects:
F^{\prime}
,
F^{\prime\prime}
,
F^{\prime\prime\prime}
,
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
,
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
,
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}
, as follows.

•

F^{\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\kappa+}
:
\mathfrak{M}
is
\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
, its coordinate functions are at most
\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
, so
F^{\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\kappa}
is optimal.

•

F^{\prime\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
: This is due to the sharpness of real Frobenius theorem that
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
only guarantees
F^{\prime\prime\prime}
to be
\mathscr{C}^{\beta}
. See Proposition
7.1
below.

•

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+}
:
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}
spans
\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}}
which is
\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
. If
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+}
then
\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma+}
as well.

•

F^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
: The argument is similar to the sharpness of real Frobenius theorem. See Proposition
7.2
below.

•

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
: This is the counter intuitive part in our results. See Section
7.2
for the complete deduction and Proposition
7.3
, Theorem
7.11
for the proof.

When
\gamma\geq\alpha+1
(that
\mathcal{S}\cap\bar{\mathcal{S}}
is at least 1 order more regular than
\mathcal{S}
), by Theorem
6.2
, all six regularity estimates above are sharp.

When
\alpha\leq\gamma<\alpha+1
, from Theorem
1.1
we only have
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
, the rest of five estimates are still sharp.

When
\alpha\leq\gamma<\alpha+1
and
\gamma>2
, using Theorem
6.1
we can find a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-coordinate chart
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime})
to
\mathbb{R}^{r}_{t}\times\mathbb{C}^{m}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}_{s}
such that
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
,
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
but
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha-}
. If we use Theorem
6.2
, then we can get
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
which is sharp, but neither
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-1}
,
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\in\mathscr{C}^{(\gamma-1)-}
nor
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma-2}
is sharp.

We do not know whether we can choose suitable coordinate chart
F
so that
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\in\mathscr{C}^{\gamma}
and
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
holds in the same time.

7.1
Sharpness of
F^{\prime\prime}
,
F^{\prime\prime\prime}
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}
: some elementary examples

In this subsection we use the conventions
f_{u}=\partial_{u}f
,
f_{v}=\partial_{v}f
, etc.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\beta>1
, and let
(u,v)
be the standard coordinate system for
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. We define
\mathcal{V}\leq T\mathbb{R}^{2}
as

\textstyle\mathcal{V}:=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{R}}T,\quad T:=\frac{\partial}{\partial u}+f(v)\frac{\partial}{\partial v},\quad\text{where }f(v):=\max(0,v)^{\beta}.

Suppose
F=(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{2}_{u,v}\to\mathbb{R}_{x}\times\mathbb{R}_{s}
is a
C^{1}
-coordinate chart near
(0,0)
such that
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
spans
\mathcal{V}|_{U}
, then
\partial_{v}F^{\prime\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\beta-1)+}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
. In particular
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{R}^{2})
.

Note that
\mathcal{V}
has rank 1 so is automatically involutive.

Proof.

By direct computation

e^{tT}(u,v)=\begin{cases}\big(u+t,\frac{v}{\left(1-(\beta-1)v^{\beta-1}t\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta-1}}}\big)&\text{for }v>0\text{ and }-\infty<t<(\beta-1)v^{1-\beta},\\
(u+t,v)&\text{for }v\leq 0\text{ and }t\in\mathbb{R}.\end{cases}

First we show that for every
\delta\neq 0
, the function

g^{\delta}:I_{\delta}\to\mathbb{R},\quad g^{\delta}(v):=\begin{cases}\frac{v}{\left(1-(\beta-1)\delta v^{\beta-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta-1}}},&v>0,\\
v,&v\leq 0,\end{cases}\quad\text{where }I_{\delta}=\begin{cases}\left(-\infty,(\frac{\delta}{\beta-1})^{\frac{1}{1-\beta}}\right),&\delta>0,\\
\mathbb{R},&\delta<0,\end{cases}

(7.1)

is not
\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
near
v=0
. Note that
e^{uT}(0,v)=(u,g^{u}(v))
for every
u
.

Indeed by Taylor’s expansion,

\frac{v}{\left(1-(\beta-1)\delta v^{\beta-1}\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta-1}}}=v+\delta v^{\beta}+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}{-\frac{1}{\beta-1}\choose k}\left(-(\beta-1)\delta\right)^{k}\cdot v^{k\beta-k+1},\quad\text{converging for }0\leq v<(\tfrac{\delta}{\beta-1})^{\frac{1}{1-\beta}}.

Hence
g^{\delta}(v)=v+\max(0,v)^{\beta}+\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}{-\frac{1}{\beta-1}\choose k}\left(-(\beta-1)\delta\right)^{k}\cdot\max(0,v)^{k\beta-k+1}
holds near
v=0
.

Now near
v=0

\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}{-\frac{1}{\beta-1}\choose k}\left(-(\beta-1)\delta\right)^{k}\cdot\max(0,v)^{k\beta-k+1}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{2\beta-1}_{\mathrm{loc}}\subsetneq\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}_{\mathrm{loc}}
-function, but
\max(0,v)^{\beta}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
. So
g^{\delta}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
near
v=0
.
Therefore the function
(u,v)\mapsto g^{u}(v)
is not
\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
near
(u,v)=(0,0)
.

By assumption
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial x}
spans
\mathcal{V}|_{U}
. So
\mathcal{V}^{\bot}|_{U}=\operatorname{Span}dF^{\prime\prime}
, which means that we have a transport equation

\textstyle TF^{\prime\prime\prime}(u,v)=0\quad\text{i.e.}\quad\partial_{u}F^{\prime\prime\prime}(u,v)=-f(v)\cdot\partial_{v}F^{\prime\prime\prime}(u,v),\quad\text{for all }(u,v)\in U.

(7.2)

Since
F
is a
C^{1}
-chart, we have
(F_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0,0),F_{v}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0,0))\neq(0,0)
. By (
7.2
) and
f(0)=0
, we have
F_{u}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0,0)=0
. Thus
F_{v}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0,0)\neq 0
.

Therefore
h(v):=F^{\prime\prime\prime}(0,v)
is a
C^{1}
-function that has non-vanishing derivatives in a neighborhood of
v=0
. By the Inverse Function Theorem, on a smaller neighborhood of
v=0
,
h
is a
C^{1}
-diffeomorphism onto its image.

Suppose by contrast
F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{(\beta-1)+}
in a neighborhood of
(u,v)=(0,0)
. By (
7.2
) since
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta}_{\mathrm{loc}}\subset\mathscr{C}^{(\beta-1)+}
, we have
F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{u}\in\mathscr{C}^{(\beta-1)+}
as well, which means
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
near
(u,v)=(0,0)
. Since
h(v)=F^{\prime\prime\prime}(0,v)
, we have
h\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
in the domain and by the Inverse Function Theorem
h^{\mathrm{Inv}}\in\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
.

So
h^{\mathrm{Inv}}\circ F^{\prime\prime}(u,v)
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
-function defined in a neighborhood of
(u,v)=(0,0)
. By (
7.2
) and (
7.1
),
F^{\prime\prime\prime}(u,v)=F^{\prime\prime\prime}(e^{-uT}(u,v))=F^{\prime\prime\prime}(0,g^{-u}(v))
holds a neighborhood of
0
.
So
h^{\mathrm{Inv}}\circ F^{\prime\prime\prime}(u,v)=g^{-u}(v)
, contradicting to the fact that
(u,v)\mapsto g^{u}(v)
is not
\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
near
(0,0)
. This concludes that
F_{v}^{\prime\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\beta-1)+}
near
(u,v)=(0,0)
.
Therefore
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\beta+}
and we finish the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.2
.

Endow
\mathbb{R}^{3}
with standard coordinate system
(u,v,\theta)
. Let
\alpha>0
. We define
\mathcal{S}\leq\mathbb{C}T\mathbb{R}^{3}
as

\mathcal{S}:=\operatorname{Span}_{\mathbb{C}}Z,\quad Z:=\partial_{u}+ie^{f(\theta)}\partial_{v}\quad\text{where }f(\theta):=\max(0,\theta)^{\alpha}.

Suppose
F=(F^{\prime\prime},F^{\prime\prime\prime}):U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{3}_{u,v,\theta}\to\mathbb{C}^{1}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}_{s}
is a
C^{1}
-coordinate chart near
0\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
such that that
F(0)=(0,0)
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
spans
\mathcal{S}|_{U}
. Then both
F^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
near
(0,0,0)
.

By assumption
Z\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
,
\mathcal{S}
is involutive since it has rank 1, and
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}=\operatorname{Span}(\frac{\partial}{\partial u},\frac{\partial}{\partial v})
is involutive as well. So
\mathcal{S}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-complex Frobenius structure.

Proof.

By assumption
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}|_{U}=\operatorname{Span}(\partial_{u},\partial_{v})|_{U}=\operatorname{Span}(F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z},F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial\bar{z}})=(\operatorname{Span}F^{*}ds)^{\bot}=(\operatorname{Span}dF^{\prime\prime\prime})^{\bot}
, so
\partial_{u}F^{\prime\prime\prime}=\partial_{v}F^{\prime\prime\prime}=0
which means
F^{\prime\prime\prime}(u,v,\theta)=F^{\prime\prime\prime}(\theta)
. So
dF^{\prime\prime\prime}=F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\theta}d\theta
and
F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\theta}(\theta)
is non-vanishing in a neighborhood of
\theta=0
.

Now
\mathcal{S}^{\bot}|_{U}=(\operatorname{Span}Z)^{\bot}|_{U}=\operatorname{Span}(F^{*}d\bar{z},F^{*}ds)=\operatorname{Span}(d\bar{F}^{\prime\prime},dF^{\prime\prime\prime})
, so

Z\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}=\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{u}+ie^{f(\theta)}\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{v}=0,\quad\Rightarrow\quad F^{\prime\prime}_{v}=-ie^{-f(\theta)}F^{\prime\prime}_{u},\quad\text{in }U.

Here we use
F^{\prime\prime}_{u}=\partial_{u}F^{\prime\prime}
,
F^{\prime\prime}_{v}=\partial_{v}F^{\prime\prime}
and
F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}=\partial_{\theta}F^{\prime\prime}
. So

\begin{pmatrix}F^{*}dz\\
F^{*}d\bar{z}\\
F^{*}ds\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}F^{\prime\prime}_{u}&-F^{\prime\prime}_{u}&F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}\\
\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{u}&\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{u}&\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}\\
&&F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\theta}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}du\\
-ie^{-f(\theta)}dv\\
d\theta\end{pmatrix}\ \Rightarrow\ \begin{pmatrix}\partial_{u}\\
ie^{f(\theta)}\partial_{v}\\
\partial_{\theta}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}F^{\prime\prime}_{u}&\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{u}&\\
-F^{\prime\prime}_{u}&\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{u}\\
F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}&\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}&F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\theta}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}F^{*}\partial_{z}\\
F^{*}\partial_{\bar{z}}\\
F^{*}\partial_{s}\end{pmatrix}.

(7.3)

Applying Cramer’s rule for inverse matrices to the right hand equation of (
7.3
), we have

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}=\frac{1}{F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\theta}(\theta)}\bigg(\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}-\frac{\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}}{\bar{F}^{\prime\prime}_{u}}\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial u}+ie^{f(\theta)}\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\Big)-\frac{F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}}{F^{\prime\prime}_{u}}\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial u}-ie^{f(\theta)}\frac{\partial}{\partial v}\Big)\bigg),\quad\text{in }U.

(7.4)

In particular we see that
F^{\prime\prime}_{u}
and
F^{\prime\prime}_{v}=-ie^{-f}F^{\prime\prime}_{u}
are both non-vanishing in the domain
U
.

For fixed
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
,
\partial_{u}+ie^{f(\theta)}\partial_{v}
is scaled
\overline{\partial}
-operator on
\mathbb{R}^{2}_{u,v}
that annihilates
F^{\prime\prime}(\cdot,\theta_{0})
. Therefore by a scaling of the Cauchy Integral Formula, for every
\tilde{V}^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime}\Subset U
,
(u,v)\in\tilde{V}^{\prime}
,
\theta\in V^{\prime\prime}
and
k\geq 0
,

F^{\prime\prime}(u,v,\theta)=\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial V^{\prime}}\frac{F^{\prime\prime}(\xi,\eta,\theta)(d\xi+ie^{-f(\theta)}d\eta)}{(\xi-u)+ie^{-f(\theta)}(\eta-v)},\quad\frac{\partial^{k}F^{\prime\prime}(u,v,\theta)}{\partial u^{k}}=\frac{k!}{2\pi i}\int_{\partial V^{\prime}}\frac{F^{\prime\prime}(\xi,\eta,\theta)(d\xi+ie^{-f(\theta)}d\eta)}{((\xi-u)+ie^{-f(\theta)}(\eta-v))^{k+1}}.

Since
F^{\prime\prime}\in C^{0}(\tilde{V}^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C})
, for every precompact subset
V^{\prime}\Subset\tilde{V}^{\prime}
we have

\|\partial_{u}^{k}F^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}^{2^{k}})}\leq k!\operatorname{dist}(V^{\prime},\partial\tilde{V}^{\prime})^{-k-1}\|F^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(\tilde{V}^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C})}<\infty,\quad\forall k\geq 0.

Using
F_{v}^{\prime\prime}=-ie^{-f(\theta)}F_{u}^{\prime\prime}
, we see that
\|\nabla_{u,v}^{k}F^{\prime\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C}^{2^{k}})}<\infty
for every
V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime}\Subset U
, which means
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{u,v}L^{\infty}_{\theta}(V^{\prime},V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C})
.

Let
\mu>0
be such that
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
. Since
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{u,v}L^{\infty}_{\theta}(V^{\prime},V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C})
for every
V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime}\Subset U
, we have:

(7.
\mu
)

For every
\mu>0
, if
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U)
, then
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\infty,\mu-}_{(u,v),s}(V^{\prime},V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C})
holds for all
V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime}\Subset U
.

Now suppose by contrast that
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{C})
. Say
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+2\varepsilon}_{\mathrm{loc}}(U;\mathbb{C})
for some
\varepsilon>0
. Take
\mu=\alpha+\varepsilon
in
(7.

μ

)
, we have
F^{\prime\prime}_{u},F^{\prime\prime}_{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+\varepsilon}(V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime};\mathbb{C})
for every precompact neighborhood
V^{\prime}\times V^{\prime\prime}\Subset U
of
0\in\mathbb{R}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\theta}
.

Since
F^{\prime\prime}_{u},F^{\prime\prime}_{v}
are both non-vanishing, we have
-ie^{f(\theta)}=F^{\prime\prime}_{v}/F_{u}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+\varepsilon}
near
(0,0,0)
. However
-ie^{f}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}
near
\theta=0
since
f\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}
near
\theta=0
. Contradiction!

This concludes the proof of
F^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}
near
(0,0,0)
.

To show
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
near
(0,0,0)
, we can assume
F^{\prime\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}
, since otherwise
F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\theta}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
in a neighborhood of
\theta=0
and by (
7.4
) the
\frac{\partial}{\partial\theta}
-component of
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}
is not
\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
already.

Let
\mu>0
be the largest number such that
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu-}
near
(0,0,0)
. By assumption
F^{\prime\prime}\in C^{1}
so
\mu\geq 1
. Since
F^{\prime\prime}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha+}
near
(0,0,0)
, we see that
\mu\leq\alpha
.

By
(7.

μ

)
, we know
F^{\prime\prime}_{u},F^{\prime\prime}_{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu-\varepsilon}
near
(0,0,0)
for every
\varepsilon>0
, in particular
F^{\prime\prime}_{u},F^{\prime\prime}_{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu-\frac{1}{2}}
near
(0,0,0)
.

We see that
F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\mu-1)+}
near
(0,0,0)
. Suppose otherwise, i.e.
F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu-1+\varepsilon}
for some
0<\varepsilon<\frac{1}{2}
. Since
F^{\prime\prime}_{u},F^{\prime\prime}_{v}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu-\frac{1}{2}}
, we have
F^{\prime\prime}\in\mathscr{C}^{\mu+\varepsilon}
. This contradicts to the maximality of
\mu
.

Now we have
F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}/F^{\prime\prime}_{u}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\mu-1)+}
for such
\mu\geq 1
, in particular
F^{\prime\prime}_{\theta}/F^{\prime\prime}_{u}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
. Since
Z=\partial_{u}+ie^{f}\partial_{v}
and
\bar{Z}=\partial_{u}-ie^{f}\partial_{v}
are both
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-vector fields in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and we already assumed
F^{\prime\prime\prime}_{\theta}\in\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
, using (
7.4
) again we conclude that
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\notin\mathscr{C}^{(\alpha-1)+}
near
(0,0,0)
. This completes the whole proof.
∎

7.2
Sharpness of
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
: a
\bar{\partial}
-equation with parameter

In this subsection we will construct a rank-1 complex Frobenius structure
\mathcal{S}
on
\mathbb{C}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}
, such that
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
whenever
F
is a coordinate chart representing
\mathcal{S}
near the origin.

We sketch the idea to the proof of Theorem
1.3
here. It is adapted from
[
Yao21
]
, where we prove that there is a
C^{k}
-integrable almost complex structure that does not admit a
C^{k+1}
-coordinate chart.

Endow
\mathbb{C}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}
with standard coordinate system
(w,\theta)
. We consider a subbundle
\mathcal{S}\leq\mathbb{C}T(\mathbb{C}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1})
as

\mathcal{S}:=\operatorname{Span}(\partial_{w}+a(w,\theta)\partial_{\bar{w}}),\quad\text{where }a\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}(\mathbb{C}^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{1};\mathbb{C})\text{ such that }\|a\|_{L^{\infty}}<1.

Clearly
\mathcal{S}
is involutive and
\mathcal{S}+\bar{\mathcal{S}}=\operatorname{Span}(\partial_{w},\partial_{\bar{w}})=(\mathbb{C}T\mathbb{C}_{w}^{1})\times\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\theta}
since
\operatorname{rank}\mathcal{S}=1
and
\sup_{w,\theta}|a(w,\theta)|<1
. So
\mathcal{S}
is a
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
-complex Frobenius structure.

Step 1
(Proposition
7.3
): We show that, the existence of a coordinate chart
F
near
(0,0)
such that
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
spans
\mathcal{S}
, is equivalent to the existence of
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
near
(w,\theta)=(0,0)
that solves

(\partial_{w}+a(w,\theta)\partial_{\bar{w}})f=-\partial_{\bar{w}}a,\quad\text{or equivalently}\quad f_{w}+(af)_{\bar{w}}-a_{\bar{w}}f=-a_{\bar{w}}.

(7.5)

Step 2
(Proposition
7.9
): We construct an
a(w,\theta)
such that
a
is smooth away from
\{w=0\}=\{0\}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\theta}
, and the complex Hilbert transform (also known as the Beurling transform)
\partial_{w}^{-1}\partial_{\bar{w}}a\notin\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
near
w=0
.

Step 3
(Theorem
7.11
): We show that there is no
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
defined near
(w,\theta)=(0,0)
that solves (
7.5
). This can be done by showing that:

•

For every
f
that solves the homogeneous equation
(\partial_{w}+a\partial_{\bar{w}})f=0
with
f(0,\theta)=0
, we must have
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
.

•

There exists a non-
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
function
f
such that
f(0,\theta)=0
and
f
solves (
7.5
).

Thus, by the superposition principle, every solution to (
7.5
) cannot be
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}
. By Step 1 we complete the proof.

To make the result more general, we consider the case of mixed regularity, where
a\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}_{w,\theta}
for arbitrary
\alpha>\frac{1}{2}
and
\beta>0
(recall again from Lemma
2.12
that
\mathscr{C}^{\alpha}_{(w,\theta)}=\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\alpha}_{w,\theta}
). See Theorem
7.12
.

We now start the proof. We use the notation
f_{w}=\partial_{w}f
etc.

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
\alpha>\frac{1}{2},\beta>0
, let
a\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{C}^{1}_{w},\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\theta};\mathbb{C})
be such that
\sup|a|<1
, and let
\mathcal{S}=\operatorname{Span}(\partial_{w}+a(w,\theta)\partial_{\bar{w}})
be a
\mathscr{C}^{\min(\alpha,\beta)}
-complex Frobenius structure with rank
1
. The following
(A)
and
(B)
are equivalent:

(A)

There is a continuous map
F:U_{1}\times V_{1}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{1}_{w}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\theta}\to\mathbb{C}^{1}_{z}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}_{s}
near
(0,0)
such that

(A.1)

F
is homeomorphic to its image,
\nabla_{z}F\in C^{0}(U_{1}\times V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2})
and
\nabla_{w}(F^{\mathrm{Inv}})\in C^{0}(U_{1}\times V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{2\times 2})
.

(A.2)

F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(U_{1},V_{1};\mathbb{C}^{3})
and
F^{*}\frac{\partial}{\partial z}
spans
\mathcal{S}|_{U_{1}\times V_{1}}
.

(B)

There is a function
f\in\mathscr{C}^{\alpha,\beta}(U_{2},V_{2};\mathbb{C})
defined in a neighborhood
U_{2}\times V_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{C}^{1}_{w}\times\mathbb{R}^{1}_{\theta}
of
(0,0)
that solves (
7.5
).

The results are also true for
